# M5 Operations, Privacy and Production Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make the platform operable, private and deployable: the §17.6 alert catalogue delivered through ntfy, the healthchecks dead-man switch and LangSmith (EU, hidden I/O); the §20 deletion machinery (fences, typed manifests and their shipping, job tombstoning, the profile purge, the Adzuna purge, delete-everything, export) with the privacy routes and UI moved from M4 (M4 Deviation 1); and production (the prod compose, Caddy/TLS, the no-egress firewall, per-service secrets, restic backups, restore with the two-phase replay and both verifications, rollback, key rotation, `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, `docs/PRIVACY.md`), proven by a full production-mode rehearsal on the compose test stack, locally and in CI (M5-U1). That covers:
- schema first: migration `0010_m5_operations.py` (the manifest, shipment, export and backup tables, `deadman_state`, the fence FK, the compatibility floor of M5-R4 R2);
- operations: the catalogue's completion with escalation and manual resolution, the never-raised codes, the ntfy dispatcher, the dead-man pings and period sync with the V15 CLI, LangSmith hardening, the §17.5 health items and the backup alert reconciler;
- privacy: the fence-coverage gaps and the global admission stop, the manifest model and its reconcilers, the backup image's shipper, the P1 batch fix, the purge framework and its five kinds, export, the privacy routes with re-authentication (M5-U4), `docs/PRIVACY.md` and the Privacy & data UI;
- production: the prod and test compose files, the Caddyfile, the secrets layout, the firewall with N1/N2 and V20, `migrate`'s flags and steps 1/3/7, backup sets, the replay, restore with C32/C37/C39 and V22, the rollback drill, rotation, the CI jobs, E18, `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, spec revision 3.7 and the acceptance.

**Architecture:**
- **The `alerts` row is the outbox.** Every alert commits with its cause (api or worker); the worker's `AlertDispatcher`, an asyncio loop beside the procrastinate loops, pushes due rows to ntfy and stamps them; the api never sends (§3.2: no egress). Backup alerts are derived by a worker reconciler from the backup tables, because the backup role cannot write `alerts` (O1).
- **One purge protocol, five kinds.** `privacy/purge.py` runs every deletion as a `purge` operation through the phases of §20.2/§19.5: A0 manifest + fence (one transaction under `active_config FOR UPDATE`), A1 quiesce, A2 content and canonical file, A3 record, B bounded chunks, C postconditions + completion + fence release, D shipped. Each kind (`privacy/kinds/*.py`) supplies its fence rows, its content computation and its chunked execution; the replay of migrate step 7 re-runs the same execution on the stored ids (D4).
- **Durability by stream.** Manifest files are canonical JSON (`format: 1`) written tmp → fsync → rename → fsync, shipped by the backup image's shipper into restic (`--tag manifests`) and marked `shipped` by a worker reconciler from the shipment items' hashes. A restore replays the whole stream in three phases with identities advanced past every bound.
- **Production stays the dev stack's shape.** `deploy/compose.yaml` carries §19.1 with the M0 dev corrections and pinned subnets; `deploy/compose.test.yaml` (`JOBHUNTER_ENV=test`) runs the same file locally and in CI with local image tags, `tls internal`, stand-ins for ntfy and healthchecks (`stubsvc`) and S3 (versitygw in its own compose project, so it survives the "VPS loss" of C32).
- **Rollback by compatibility floor.** Every revision records the oldest revision whose code still runs on its schema (`schema_compat`); an older release's `migrate` and `/readyz` accept a newer head whose floor they know, and refuse a destructive one with a named code (M5-R4 R2).
- **Testing:** pytest on the real PostgreSQL container for every route, purge kind, replay variant and reconciler (§18.2's privacy list); crash cases C26, C31, C36, C38 in process and C32, C37, C39 on the compose test stack; respx and fakes for ntfy, healthchecks and LangSmith (no external call); stack tests (`-m stack`) for N1, N2, V20, V22, the backup image and the drills; Vitest and the M4 visual protocol for every UI change; E18 on the E2E stack with a local restic repository.

**Tech Stack:**
- The M4 stack. New runtime pins: `croniter` 6.2.4 and `langsmith` 0.14.11 become direct dependencies (both already in `uv.lock` at these versions, so no new download).
- New images (digest-pinned in `deploy/images.lock`, each checked on Docker Hub on 2026-11-09):
  - `restic/restic:0.19.1@sha256:136600b6ff6843d61d355f7f71f460a166429f35de6fd11b568fece3c9a4d510` (pushed 2026-07-05; only its static `/usr/bin/restic` is copied into the backup image);
  - `versity/versitygw:v1.9.0@sha256:eb7f786fcd91e72194e48fcf1ac16302d686bf8e3a71434bdbc067a6efd3fdc7` (pushed 2026-10-13, about 36 MB; the S3 stand-in of the compose test stack only);
  - `aquasec/trivy:0.76.0@sha256:2416d1f74045b57274df1a340eee6b510cacd73c66a6ccb8da092b117eb7d222` (pushed 2026-10-14; the CI image scan only).
- The PostgreSQL 17 client of the backup image is copied from the already pinned `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg17-bookworm` image (PostgreSQL 17.11, `postgresql-client-17` `17.11-1.pgdg12+2`, read from the local image), so client and server are the same build and the build needs no package download.
- GitHub Actions new to the repository (`docker/setup-buildx-action`, `docker/login-action`, `docker/build-push-action`): each pinned to the full SHA of its current release, read at execution with `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/ref/tags/<tag>` (to verify at execution, as M4 Task 32 did for `upload-artifact`).

**Spec:** docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-job-hunter-design.md, revision 3.6, moving to **revision 3.7** (Task 32 writes every spec change this plan prescribes, with the change log in the status line). M5 covers this roadmap row of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-job-hunter-roadmap.md`: §16.9, §17.5–17.8, §19.1–19.8 (prod), §20, §2.4 C1–C3 runtime checks, and the spikes V15, V20, V21 and V22. It also implements §7.14 (fences and manifests), §7.16's manifest, export and backup machines, §14.2's privacy rows, the purge-retry row and the golden-case delete, §15.1's offsite banner, §15.7's Privacy & data and failed purges, §17.4's `manifest_shipping`, `purge_fences` and `deadman_sync`, §18.2's privacy and migrate items, §18.3's C26, C31, C32, C36–C39, and §18.7–18.8 (E18, `images`, N1, N2).

These earlier decisions bind M5 where they name it: `docs/council/m4-final-review/rulings.md` (T12-b: the replay's fence requeue; T17-b: an edge limit on login; F4-a/F4-d: the activation-preview and E2E-harness carries); `docs/council/m3-final-review/rulings.md` (P42: M5 decides L4086 vs L6452; T27-a: M5 owns the RUNBOOK; T27-d: `restart` vs `up -d`); the M4 plan's Deviations 1 and 6 and its Carry-forward 1–7; the M0–M3 Carry-forward items `.superpowers/sdd/m5-research/carries.md` lists. The user's decisions M5-U1–U4 and the controller's rulings M5-R1–R8 (plan-author brief of 2026-11-09) are binding; each is recorded below as a numbered Deviation where it departs from or adds to the spec. Where two research digests proposed different mechanisms, the Deviation names the one chosen and why.

## Global Constraints

Every Global Constraint of the M0–M4 plans still applies: exact pins (no caret in `package.json`), `mypy --strict`, ruff with the banned APIs and the `BLE001` allowlists, `pytest -W error`, injected clocks (`jobhunter.clock.utcnow`, patched per module in tests), `transition()` / `transition_where` for every state change, the §3.3.3 lock schedule with **`ranking_state` the last lock of every transaction**, JSONB binds through `q(..., jsonb=[...])`, no PII, ad text, note, CV or profile content in a log line, metric label, alert, operation payload, manifest or trace, the M4 frontend gates, SPA rules (CSP, i18n by code, `Idempotency-Key` per user action, background polls) and API rules (`require_session`, the response sets of `api/openapi.py`, `schema.d.ts` regenerated in the same commit, domain code raises `Refusal`), and Conventional Commits with the scope `m5` (`feat(m5): …`). Step-2 failure messages are indicative. Test bodies given as prose are written by the implementer from the named assertions; a vacuous or tautological body is an Important review finding (M2 Ruling P4).

M5 adds:

- **Commands.**
  - Backend: `JOBHUNTER_STACK_PREBUILT=embedder uv run --group docparse pytest -W error -q -p no:cacheprovider <paths>`, written `… pytest … <paths>` in the steps; before a commit also `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check . && uv run mypy`. A new script under `scripts/` is added to `mypy.ini`'s `files` in the task that creates it (the five legacy files of M4 Ruling T20-a stay excluded).
  - The full pytest suite in the dev container runs only in sequential chunks, each its own pytest session (`tests/unit tests/property`, then `tests/integration` in four directory-sorted quarters, then `tests/crash`), never one session.
  - Frontend gates (all four, before every frontend commit): `npm --prefix frontend run lint`, `npm --prefix frontend run typecheck`, `npm --prefix frontend test`, `npm --prefix frontend run build`; and `scripts/check-openapi-drift.sh` whenever a route or model changed.
  - The dev/E2E stack (`DC`): `docker compose --env-file deploy/images.lock -f deploy/compose.dev.yaml -f deploy/compose.fixtures.yaml -f deploy/compose.fakellm.yaml -f deploy/compose.e2e.yaml`.
  - The compose test stack (`PT`, from Task 21): `docker compose -p jobhunter-prodtest --env-file deploy/images.lock --env-file deploy/compose.test.env -f deploy/compose.yaml -f deploy/compose.test.yaml`; its restore target (`PB`) is the same with `-p jobhunter-prodtest-b`; the S3 stand-in (`S3`): `docker compose -p jobhunter-s3stub --env-file deploy/images.lock -f deploy/compose.s3stub.yaml`.
  - Stack tests: `JOBHUNTER_STACK_PREBUILT=embedder uv run pytest -m stack -q -p no:cacheprovider <paths>`. The dev stack, the M4 `jobhunter-stacktest` project and the two prod-test projects all use `172.30.0.0/24`: only one runs at a time, and each stack fixture skips with a message when another is up (the `tests/stack/test_dev_stack.py` pattern); so `PB` comes up only after `$PT down`. `S3` starts before either (its network `jobhunter-s3net` is external to them and must exist first) and is removed last, by `tests/support/prodstack.prod_session` (Task 21).
- **Visual verification (roadmap gate 5).** Every task that changes the UI ends with the M4 plan's protocol (M4 Global Constraints) against the dev stack with the SPA rebuilt, screenshots to `.superpowers/sdd/m5-visual/task-<N>/` (never committed): each changed view at 1280×800, 390×844 and 200 % zoom, in English and German for text-heavy views, the console free of CSP errors; the report lists every view, size and language checked.
- **No paid call anywhere in M5.** ntfy, healthchecks, LangSmith and S3 are stand-ins or fakes in every test: respx for ntfy and healthchecks in process, `stubsvc` (`tests/stack/stubs/http_recorder.py`) and versitygw on the compose test stack, LangSmith's transforms called directly on a client that is never given a network. `LANGSMITH_TRACING` is unset or `false` everywhere but `deploy/compose.yaml`'s prod environment.
- **Disk.** The dev container has about 0.4–0.7 GB free on `/`. Before every stack step run `df -h / && docker system df` and stop, reporting the figures, when the free space is below the step's named expected cost plus 300 MB (the build cache and logs need room too). Stack steps reuse the existing images: the compose test stack runs `jobhunter-app:dev`, `jobhunter-docparse:dev`, `jobhunter-embedder:e2e` (the fake target) and the new `jobhunter-backup:dev`, whose stage adds about 45 MB to the app image (restic about 30 MB, the PostgreSQL client and its nine libraries about 12 MB); versitygw adds about 36 MB, the python image (already present) serves `stubsvc` and the N1/N2 probes. Each stack step names its expected cost; every test project removes its own containers, networks and volumes at the end (`down -v`, M5-U2) and never touches another project's. The real embedder image `jobhunter-embedder:dev` (4.84 GB) is used by no M5 step.
- **Host changes (M5-U2).** The stack tests may `sudo groupadd --gid 10010 jobhunter-secrets` when absent, write `.secrets/test/` with `sudo scripts/test-secrets.sh`, and load and delete the nft table `inet jobhunter_egress` with `sudo nft`; they remove the nft table at the end and never change another table.
- **Secrets.** No step prints, logs or copies a secret value; `.secrets/` is read only by the scripts and services that mount it. Test and rehearsal secrets are dummy values made by `scripts/test-secrets.sh`; the stand-ins record whether an `Authorization` or `X-Api-Key` header was present, never its value.
- **Fixtures.** Every fixture and seed runs on the real schema with its CHECKs, unique and partial-unique indexes and triggers enabled, and moves rows only along the transition matrix's edges (Task 13's seed rule lists the singletons M5 meets). A scenario that needs two rows a singleton forbids, such as two live batch submissions or two running exports, is parametrized over separate databases.
- **Manifests and exports.** A manifest (file, `scope`, log line, alert) holds ids, table and column names, selectors and key hashes only. An export holds the user's content and exists only encrypted at rest with the data key.
- **Stack resets (M5-U2).** `docker compose -p <project> down -v` and `docker volume rm` are allowed on the projects the step created and on `jobhunter-dev`; no step removes an image it did not build in that step (except `jobhunter-app:prev`, which Task 27 creates and removes).

## Review Focus

1. **A destructive request sent twice, retried, or confirmed with a wrong second factor.** A double-click on Delete everything, the same `Idempotency-Key` after the user was logged out by the purge, a new key while the purge runs, a wrong TOTP code typed five times.
   - Expected: exactly one purge operation and one manifest; the same key returns the stored 202 even after the purge finished (its own `api_idempotency` row survives step 11); a new key while it runs answers 409 `conflict_state`; each wrong password or code is a failed login attempt, so the fifth locks the address out like a login (M5-U4); after re-login the status is found through `GET /api/privacy/manifests`.
   - Pinned by Task 18 (`test_delete_everything_is_idempotent_across_the_logout`, `test_a_new_key_while_a_purge_runs_is_409`, `test_wrong_totp_codes_lock_the_address_out`) and Task 30 (E18).
2. **A restore that meets the stream's edge cases.** A manifest newer than the dump, a manifest whose file was never written (C39) or never shipped, a second `migrate` after new rows were created (C37).
   - Expected: replay inserts or completes each one before any fence is released; rows above a watermark or `max_ids` are never deleted; a later replay deletes nothing; `restore verify --phase replayed` passes only after a pending retry was completed.
   - Pinned by Task 25 (the typed-replay tests of §18.2) and Task 26 (`test_c37_newer_manifest_replays_once`, `test_c39_pending_retry_completes_before_verify`).
3. **ntfy unreachable for hours, then back.** The topic URL refuses connections from 22:00 to 06:00 while ten warn and two critical alerts open.
   - Expected: the dispatcher never blocks the loop or floods: one failing alert gets its four tries per pass, then the dispatcher pauses (5 min doubling to 60 min); one in-app `notify.delivery_failed`; once ntfy answers, every due alert goes out exactly once, criticals first, ten per pass, and the in-app alert resolves.
   - Pinned by Task 4 (`test_an_outage_pauses_and_backs_off_without_flooding`, `test_after_recovery_each_due_alert_is_sent_once_critical_first`).
4. **A purge meeting live work.** A source purge while a batch with affected targets is `submitted`, an export is `running`, the user saves an affected job's group member, and `det_completeness` runs.
   - Expected: the batch completes `reconciled` with the purged lines counted `purged` (no `batch.custom_id_anomaly`, the next batch can start); the export ends `purged`; the member save is refused with the fence's 409; no row derived from the purged source is written above the watermark.
   - Pinned by Task 11 (`test_a_purged_in_batch_target_no_longer_blocks_the_batch`), Task 8 (one test per writer), Task 17 (`test_an_export_meeting_a_fence_ends_purged`).
5. **The schedule changed, paused or overlapped by a manual run.** Interval 1 h → 24 h, the schedule disabled and re-enabled, a "Run now" covering a scheduled slot.
   - Expected: the healthchecks check carries `timeout = interval`, `grace = clamp(0.25 × interval, 1 h, 12 h)` within a tick of the save (or after the backoff when the API fails, with `deadman.update_failed`); disabling pauses it, enabling resumes it with a ping; the manual run whose window covered a skipped slot pings with its own status, so no false off-box alarm.
   - Pinned by Task 5 (`test_a_schedule_save_syncs_the_check_within_a_tick`, `test_disable_pauses_and_enable_resumes_with_a_ping`, `test_a_manual_run_covering_a_skipped_slot_pings`).

**Task order.** M5-R8's sketch, schema and infrastructure first, then operations, privacy, production, docs, spec and acceptance, with these changes, each so that a reviewer can reject one part alone or so that a later task can be verified:
- the catalogue is two tasks: the catalogue data, escalation and manual resolution (Task 2), and the raisers and resolvers of individual codes (Task 3);
- the §17.5 health items and the backup alert reconciler are their own operations task (Task 7): the tables exist from Task 1, and O1 makes them worker-side;
- the backup image's base and the backup and restore startup roles move forward to the shipper (Task 10): the shipper is the backup image's code and E18 needs it on the dev stack;
- the P1 batch fix (Task 11) precedes the purge framework (Task 12): it is an M3 bug reproduced and fixed on its own, and every purge kind but the golden-case delete uses it;
- `docs/PRIVACY.md` (Task 19) precedes the privacy UI (Task 20): the SPA renders the file at build time;
- the startup roles leave sketch item 17 (they are Task 10's); replay is its own task (Task 25) between backup sets (Task 24) and restore (Task 26); rollback (Task 27) and rotation (Task 28) are two tasks.

### Task 1: Migration 0010, the M5 tables, the transition specs and the compatibility floor

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/db/migrations/versions/0010_m5_operations.py`, `backend/src/jobhunter/db/compat.py`, `tests/support/fences.py`, `tests/integration/test_migration_0010.py`, `tests/unit/test_compat_floors.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/db/transitions.py` (four new specs and the `query_proposals` purge edges), `backend/src/jobhunter/db/migrate.py` (the `alembic` step records the floors), `backend/src/jobhunter/db/seed.py` (the `deadman_state` row), `backend/src/jobhunter/auth/throttle.py` (a lockout write takes a new generation), `tests/unit/test_transition_registry.py`, `tests/integration/test_migrate.py`; the fence fixtures that insert `purge_fences` with a literal `manifest_id`, which the new foreign key refuses: `tests/support/scoring.py` (`_FENCE_JOBS`, `_FENCE_PROFILE`) and `tests/integration/test_{admission_candidates,api_job_detail,api_labelling,atomic_claim,batch_submit,cycles,embed_profile,fenced_items,fences_m1,finalize,is_fenced_profile,merge_unmerge,profile_confirm,publish,quota_ledger,replay_operation,schema,spend_ledger}.py`; `tests/support/fixtures.py`, whose per-test reset empties a fixed table list (`TRUNCATED_TABLES` `:60`, `reset_database` `:319`) and would otherwise carry manifests, exports and backup rows from one test into the next: `TRUNCATED_TABLES` gains `deletion_manifests`, `manifest_shipments`, `manifest_shipment_items`, `backup_sets`, `backup_runs`, `exports` and `purged_batch_items` (no referencing path from them reaches the configuration), `reset_database` restores the `deadman_state` row to its seeded NULLs as it resets `schedule_state`, and `schema_compat` (migrate's record) is kept; `tests/integration/test_clean_db.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: §7.14's DDL (L2745–2865), verbatim; `db/migrate.py:29-46` (`MigrateStep`, `MIGRATE_ORDER`), `apply_explicit_grants` with `BACKUP_GRANT_TABLES` (`db/migrate.py:48, 63`), which grants on the four backup tables once they exist; `TransitionSpec` (`db/transitions.py:41`), the `query_proposals` spec (`db/transitions.py:514`, edges `running → ready | failed` only) and the `assert_transition` trigger whose matrix `migrate` syncs from the registry (`seed.sync_transition_matrix`, `db/seed.py:344`); `seed_defaults` (`db/seed.py:368`); `throttle._LOCK_OUT` (`auth/throttle.py:64`).
- Produces:
  - The tables `deletion_manifests`, `manifest_shipments`, `manifest_shipment_items`, `backup_sets`, `backup_runs`, `exports` exactly as §7.14, plus `exports.size_bytes bigint CHECK (size_bytes >= 0)` and `exports.purge_generation bigint` (Deviation 19), `api_idempotency.id bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` with `UNIQUE (id)`: the table's key stays `(route, idem_key)` (`0001_m0_baseline.py:120-130`), the existing rows are numbered, and the surrogate gives it the one identity column that `max_identity_ids` and a `keep` id need (Deviation 24). The table is small, its rows living 7 days. Also `deletion_manifests.request jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` (the purge's request, ids only: what `compute` needs, kept with the manifest so no operation has to carry it; Deviation 53) and `llm_calls.content_purged_at timestamptz` (the marker of a `field_redact llm_calls output_json`, which settlement honours; Deviation 54); `ALTER TABLE purge_fences ADD FOREIGN KEY (manifest_id) REFERENCES deletion_manifests(id)` (M0 Deviation 1); the `assert_transition` trigger on the four state tables.
  - `purged_batch_items (submission_id bigint NOT NULL REFERENCES batch_submissions(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, custom_id text NOT NULL, manifest_id bigint NOT NULL REFERENCES deletion_manifests(id), purged_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), PRIMARY KEY (submission_id, custom_id))`: the content-free evidence that a purge deleted an item of a live submission (Task 11; Deviation 13).
  - `login_lockouts.generation bigint GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY` (existing rows numbered), and `_LOCK_OUT`'s `ON CONFLICT` sets `generation = DEFAULT`, so every lock or renewal carries a new value: the table has no other identity, parent or `created_at` that a manifest could bound it by, and its key holds an IP address (Deviation 16).
  - Indexes: `deletion_manifests_open (status, completed_at) WHERE status <> 'shipped'`; `manifest_shipment_items_uuid (manifest_uuid, file_sha256)`; `backup_sets_latest (finished_at DESC) WHERE status = 'succeeded'`; `backup_runs_latest (kind, finished_at DESC)`; `UNIQUE exports_one_running ((true)) WHERE status = 'running'` (one live export, D9).
  - `deadman_state (id smallint PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id = 1), applied_interval_s integer, applied_timeout_s integer, applied_grace_s integer, applied_paused boolean, last_pinged_run_id bigint, synced_at timestamptz, last_error_code text, next_try_at timestamptz)`, its row seeded (all NULL) by `seed_defaults` (Task 5 uses it); `worker_heartbeat.tracing jsonb` (nullable; Task 6 writes it).
  - `schema_compat (revision text PRIMARY KEY, compat_floor text NOT NULL, destructive boolean NOT NULL, recorded_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now())`.
  - Registry specs: `deletion_manifests` (`status`; edges `executing → completed → shipped`; changed-at `completed_at`, `shipped_at`; settable `file_sha256`, `scope`, `shipment_id`, `replay_log`); `exports` (`status`; edges `running → ready | failed | purged`, `ready → expired | purged`; settable `storage_path`, `sha256`, `size_bytes`, `purge_generation`); `backup_sets` (`status`, reason `error`; `running → succeeded | failed`, changed-at `finished_at`; settable `restic_snapshot_id`, `dump_sha256`, `dump_bytes`, `db_snapshot_at`, `manifest_count`, `row_counts`, `cv_file_count`); `backup_runs` (as `backup_sets`, settable `report`); `query_proposals` gains `running → purged`, `ready → purged`, `failed → purged` (the profile purge, Task 14; §7.5's `purged` status had no edge), with `items` settable in the same update.
  - `db/compat.py`: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CompatInfo: revision: str; compat_floor: str; destructive: bool`; `def chain_compat() -> tuple[CompatInfo, ...]` (every revision of the package chain, base first, from each migration module's `compat_floor: Final[str]` and `destructive: Final[bool]`; a module without them, 0001–0009, has its own revision as floor and `destructive = False`; `ValueError` when a floor is not an ancestor-or-self of its revision); `async def record_compat(conn: AsyncConnection) -> int` (upserts every row, idempotent; the `alembic` step calls it right after `upgrade_head`). 0010 declares `compat_floor = "0009_m4_ui"`, `destructive = False`. Task 27 reads the table.
  - `tests/support/fences.py`: `TEST_MANIFEST_SQL: Final` (an `INSERT INTO deletion_manifests (manifest_uuid, kind, status) VALUES (gen_random_uuid(), :kind, 'executing') RETURNING id`, for the synchronous schema tests); `async def insert_test_manifest(conn, *, kind: str) -> int`; `async def insert_test_fence(conn, *, kind: Literal["global", "source", "profile", "jobs"], source_id: str | None = None, profile_version_id: int | None = None, job_ids: Sequence[int] = (), released: bool = False) -> int` (a manifest of the matching kind — `delete_everything`, `source_purge`, `profile_purge`, `job_tombstone` — then the fence with its id; the fence's id). Every fixture listed in Files uses it instead of a literal `manifest_id`; `test_schema.py`'s CHECK-violation cases keep their literal id (a CHECK is checked before the foreign key's trigger, so they still fail with 23514).

  - `raise_alert` escalates (O7): when the open row's severity ranks below the new one (`info < warn < critical`), the row takes the new severity, message and context and `last_notified_at := NULL` (so the dispatcher pushes at once); a lower one never de-escalates; `occurrences` and `last_at` move as before.

  - `acknowledge_alert` also sets `resolved_at` for a code in `RESOLVE_ON_ACK`, so the next violation opens a new row that pauses the lane and pushes again (O6, Q13).
  - `AlertOut` gains `last_notified_at: UtcDatetime | None`, `notify_failures: int`, `manual: bool` (O22d).
  - `POST /api/alerts/{alert_id}/resolve` → 200 `AlertOut`; not keyed (as `/ack`); its row in `tests/unit/test_openapi_m5.py`'s `M5_ROUTES` (`("post", "/api/alerts/{alert_id}/resolve"): {"401", "403", "404", "409", "422", "429"}`), which this task creates with `test_the_m4_rows_of_the_document_are_unchanged` and the M4 file's description check.
  - System → Alerts: a "Resolve" button on unresolved rows whose `manual` is true, behind a confirmation dialog; each row shows "Notified <time>" or "Delivery failed n×". EN/DE text for every catalogue code (`alert.<code>`).

### Task 4: The ntfy dispatcher

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/observability/ntfy.py`, `tests/unit/test_ntfy.py`, `tests/integration/test_alert_dispatcher.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/config.py`, `worker/main.py` (start and stop), `worker/runtime.py` (`dispatcher` field), `tests/unit/test_config.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `CATALOGUE`, `push_priority`, `NEVER_PUSHED` (Task 2); the active settings' `notifications.push_min_severity` (`settings/model.py:506`, §13.1 L4909); `epoch.fence_epoch`; the `ntfy_token` secret; `cfg.public_origin`; `ENV_PREFIXES` (`config.py:55`).
- Produces:
  - `AppConfig.ntfy_topic_url: str | None` (env `NTFY_TOPIC_URL`) and `AppConfig.healthchecks_api_url: str` (env `HEALTHCHECKS_API_URL`, default `DEFAULT_HEALTHCHECKS_API_URL: Final = "https://healthchecks.io/api/v3"`, verified at the deployment step's V15): `load_config` reads these two exact names besides the prefixes; the topic URL is registered for log masking; the startup check `ntfy_in_prod` (a prod worker needs an https `NTFY_TOPIC_URL`) runs after `healthchecks_in_prod` (O4, Deviation 11).
  - `NTFY_RETRY_DELAYS_S: Final = (5.0, 30.0, 120.0)` (four tries), `DISPATCH_INTERVAL_S: Final = 30.0`, `MAX_SENDS_PER_PASS: Final = 10`, `RENOTIFY_AFTER: Final[Mapping[Literal["warn", "critical"], timedelta]]` = 6 h / 1 h, `PAUSE_FIRST: Final = timedelta(minutes=5)`, `PAUSE_MAX: Final = timedelta(minutes=60)`, `SEND_TIMEOUT_S: Final = 10.0`, `DELIVERY_FAILED: Final = "notify.delivery_failed"`.
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class DueAlert: id: int; code: str; severity: Literal["warn", "critical"]; message: str`; `def ntfy_headers(alert: DueAlert, *, public_origin: str, token: str | None) -> dict[str, str]` → `Title: Job Hunter - <code>` (ASCII hyphen, O3), `Priority: 5 | 3`, `Tags: <severity>`, `Click: <public_origin>/settings#alerts` (O22a), and `Authorization: Bearer <token>` only with a token.
  - `class NtfyClient: __init__(self, http: httpx.AsyncClient, *, topic_url: str, token: str | None, public_origin: str)`; `async def publish(self, alert: DueAlert) -> None` (`POST topic_url`, body the alert's `message` in UTF-8; any non-2xx, timeout or connect error raises `NtfyError(code)`, `code` one of `timeout`, `connect`, `http_<status>`).
  - `async def due_alerts(conn, *, now: datetime, min_severity: Literal["warn", "critical"]) -> list[DueAlert]`: unresolved, unacknowledged, severity at or above `min_severity` and never `info`, code not in `NEVER_PUSHED`, and `last_notified_at IS NULL OR last_notified_at < now − RENOTIFY_AFTER[severity]`; criticals first, then `first_at`, `id`; at most `MAX_SENDS_PER_PASS`.
  - `class AlertDispatcher: __init__(self, engine, client: NtfyClient, *, epoch: int, clock, sleep)`; `async def pass_once(self) -> DispatchReport` (`DispatchReport(sent: int, failed_alert_id: int | None, paused_until: datetime | None)`); `async def run(self, stop: asyncio.Event) -> None`. Rules (Q1, Q2, Q4, Q5; Deviation 5): nothing while paused; each successful send is stamped `last_notified_at = now` in a transaction that starts with `fence_epoch` (a superseded worker stamps nothing, and the loop stops); the first failing alert of a pass gets its four tries, then `notify_failures + 1` on that row, `notify.delivery_failed` (subject `ntfy`) raised, the pass ends and the dispatcher pauses `PAUSE_FIRST`, doubling to `PAUSE_MAX`; the next successful send resolves it and resets the pause. A crash between a send and its stamp re-sends once (at least once). Resolution is never pushed.
  - Worker step 10 starts `AlertDispatcher.run` beside the queue loops when `cfg.ntfy_topic_url` is set (else one `ntfy_disabled` log line); the teardown stops it first.

### Task 5: The dead-man pings, the period sync and the V15 CLI

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/observability/deadman.py`, `backend/src/jobhunter/cli_ops.py`, `tests/unit/test_deadman.py`, `tests/integration/test_deadman_pings.py`, `tests/integration/test_deadman_sync.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_ops.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/graphs/collect/nodes/finalize.py` (the fast path after the run-ending commit), `worker/runs.py` (`_finalize`'s fast path), `worker/runtime.py` (`deadman: DeadmanPinger | None`), `worker/main.py`, `worker/tasks.py` (the `deadman_sync` periodic task), `config.py` (`healthchecks_ping_url_form`), `cli.py` (`ops`), `db/migrate.py` (the advisory-key comment gains 7261005), `tests/unit/test_config.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `deadman_state` (Task 1); `schedule_state(interval_s, enabled)`; `runs(trigger, scheduled_slot, status, queued_at, finished_at)`; the `healthchecks_ping_url` and `healthchecks_api_key` secrets; `cfg.healthchecks_api_url`, `NtfyClient` and `DueAlert` (Task 4); `defer_in_tx`; `finalize_from_graph` (`graphs/collect/nodes/finalize.py:382`) and `runs._finalize` (`worker/runs.py:682`).
- Produces:
  - `def grace_s(interval_s: int) -> int` = `min(max(interval_s // 4, 3600), 43200)` (§17.7).
  - `def check_uuid(ping_url: str) -> str` (the ping URL's last path segment, a UUID, else `ValueError`); startup check `healthchecks_ping_url_form` for a prod worker (O5).
  - `def ping_kind(status: str) -> Literal["success", "fail"] | None` (`succeeded`, `partial` → success; `failed`, `failed_invariant` → fail; any other → None).
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CheckState: timeout_s: int; grace_s: int; paused: bool`; `class HealthchecksClient: __init__(self, http, *, ping_url: str, api_url: str, api_key: str)`; `async def ping(self, kind) -> bool` (`GET <ping URL>` or `<ping URL>/fail`; tries at 0, 2 and 10 s with a 10 s timeout; a failure logs `deadman_ping_failed` with its error class and raises nothing; no alert: the off-box switch is the signal); `async def update_check(self, *, timeout_s: int, grace_s: int) -> None` (`POST <api>/checks/<uuid>`, JSON `{"timeout", "grace"}`, header `X-Api-Key`); `async def pause(self) -> None` (`POST <api>/checks/<uuid>/pause`); `async def read_check(self) -> CheckState` (`GET <api>/checks/<uuid>`); API failures raise `DeadmanApiError(code)`.
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class RunToPing: run_id: int; kind: Literal["success", "fail"]`; `async def runs_to_ping(conn, *, after_run_id: int) -> list[RunToPing]`: terminal runs above the watermark whose `ping_kind` is set and whose trigger is `schedule` or `catch_up`, or a `manual` run whose `[queued_at, finished_at]` window contains the `scheduled_slot` of a `skipped_overlap` run (O18, M5-R2), in id order.
  - `DEADMAN_LOCK_KEY: Final = 7261005` (a one-key session advisory lock, listed with the project's keys in `db/migrate.py`'s comment): every ping decision and every `deadman_sync` action runs holding it on a dedicated autocommit connection (no transaction is open during an HTTP call). Under it a ping first reads `schedule_state.enabled` and `deadman_state.applied_paused`: a disabled schedule or a paused check sends no ping, and the watermark still moves past the run; so a run that ends after the schedule was disabled can never resume a check `deadman_sync` paused (Deviation 9).
  - `class DeadmanPinger` with `async def ping_pending(self) -> int` (the durable path of the `deadman_ping` reconciler below, under the same lock: every run of `runs_to_ping` above the watermark pinged, the watermark moved past it; shared by that tick and delete-everything, Task 16) and `async def after_run(self, run_id: int) -> None`: the fast path, called after the run-ending commit (never inside it) by `finalize_from_graph` and `runs._finalize`; under the lock, pings when the run is in `runs_to_ping` and the check may be pinged, then `last_pinged_run_id := GREATEST(last_pinged_run_id, run_id)`.
  - Reconciler `deadman_ping` (tick): the durable path, every run of `runs_to_ping` above the watermark (this covers `resume_exhausted` and `orphaned_queue` ends and a crash between a commit and its ping); it also compares `schedule_state(interval_s, enabled)` with `deadman_state(applied_interval_s, applied_paused)` and, when they differ or `synced_at` is older than 24 h, and `next_try_at` has passed, defers `deadman_sync` (queue `aux`, queueing lock `deadman_sync`) (Q6, Q7, O2, O10).
  - Periodic task `deadman_sync` (cron `23 4 * * *`, plus the tick's defers), under `DEADMAN_LOCK_KEY`: reads the check back; enabled → applies `timeout = interval_s`, `grace = grace_s(interval_s)` when they differ, and after a pause resumes it with a `success` ping; disabled → `pause`; stamps `applied_*`, `synced_at`, clears `last_error_code`, resolves `deadman.update_failed`. A failure raises `deadman.update_failed` (warn, subject `healthchecks`), records `last_error_code` and sets `next_try_at = now + 5 min`, doubling to 60 min; pings continue.
  - Without both secrets (dev, test): no pinger, no sync, one `deadman_disabled` log line (Q8). `_REGISTERING_MODULES` gains `jobhunter.observability.deadman`.
  - `cli_ops.py` (worker role): `jobhunter ops notify-test` publishes `DueAlert(0, "test", "warn", "Job Hunter test notification")` and prints `{"sent": true}` or `{"sent": false, "error": "<code>"}` (exit 0 / 1); `jobhunter ops deadman-sync --show [--interval-hours N]` applies the period from `schedule_state` (or from N when no database is reachable), then prints `{"timeout_s", "grace_s", "paused"}` as read back. Neither prints the URL, the uuid, the token or the key (Q14; V15 runs at the deployment step, Carry-forward 1).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** `test_deadman.py` (respx): `test_grace_at_1h_4h_6h_24h_7d` (3600, 3600, 5400, 21600, 43200); `test_check_uuid_parses_the_uuid_form_and_refuses_a_slug_url`; `test_ping_kind_per_status`; `test_ping_tries_at_0_2_10_and_never_raises`; `test_update_check_sends_timeout_grace_and_the_api_key`. `test_config.py`: `test_healthchecks_ping_url_form_refuses_a_slug_url_in_prod`. `test_deadman_pings.py`: `test_scheduled_success_and_partial_ping_success`; `test_failed_and_failed_invariant_ping_fail`; `test_a_manual_run_never_pings`; `test_a_manual_run_covering_a_skipped_slot_pings`; `test_resume_exhausted_and_orphaned_queue_ping_fail_through_the_tick`; `test_a_crash_between_commit_and_ping_pings_once_from_the_tick` (fast path skipped; the next tick pings; a second tick sends nothing); `test_the_watermark_never_moves_back`. `test_deadman_sync.py`: `test_a_schedule_save_syncs_the_check_within_a_tick`; `test_disable_pauses_and_enable_resumes_with_a_ping`; `test_a_run_finishing_after_the_schedule_was_disabled_does_not_resume_the_check` (the schedule disabled while a scheduled run is in flight; the pause applied; the run's end sends no ping and the check stays paused); `test_an_api_failure_raises_update_failed_and_backs_off_5_to_60`; `test_the_daily_sync_reapplies_a_drifted_check`; `test_without_secrets_nothing_is_sent`. `test_cli_ops.py`: `test_notify_test_prints_no_secret`; `test_deadman_sync_show_prints_only_the_period`.

### Task 8: The fence-coverage gaps and the global fence as an admission stop

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/fences.py`, `backend/src/jobhunter/db/fence_guard.py`, `tests/integration/test_fence_coverage_m5.py`, `tests/integration/test_global_fence_stop.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/reconcilers.py` (`det_completeness`), `settings/scope_apply.py` (the precompute), `scoring/golden.py` (`record_feedback`, `set_flags`; its `Problem` raising becomes `Refusal`, M4 Carry-forward 3 in part), `jobs/actions.py` (group members), `dedup/groups.py` (`split_member`), `worker/embedding_reconcile.py`, `graphs/collect/nodes/embed.py`, `scoring/similarity.py`, `scoring/targets.py` and `api/routes/targets.py` (score now, retry, score individually), `jobs/listing.py`, `worker/operations.py` (`claim_operation`), `api/edge.py`, `api/app.py` (installs the guard), `api/errors.py` (`persist_unhandled_alert` outside the guard), `db/snapshot.py` (`read_snapshot` outside the guard), `errors.py`, `frontend/src/i18n/{en,de,codes}.json`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the SQL `is_fenced(job, tuple)` and `db/guards.global_fence_active` (`db/guards.py:25`); the labelling query's fence pattern (`jobs/labelling.py:82`); `claim_operation` (`worker/operations.py:422`); `EdgeMiddleware` and `csrf_failure` (`api/edge.py:104, 175`); `create_app` (`api/app.py:190`); `persist_unhandled_alert` (`api/errors.py:213`); the CV upload's transaction (`api/routes/profile.py:349-357`, `profile/upload.open_new_draft` `:457`), which takes no `active_config`; `Refusal` and `ErrorCode` (`errors.py:20`); `golden.record_feedback` and `set_flags` (`scoring/golden.py:268, 494`); `split_member` (`dedup/groups.py:876`).
- Produces:
  - `privacy/fences.py`: `async def refuse_under_global_fence(conn: AsyncConnection) -> None` → `Refusal(409, "delete_everything_in_progress")`, called by the privacy requests, which the edge and the guard below exempt, right after they lock `active_config` (`request_golden_case_delete`, `request_source_purge`, `request_export`: Tasks 12, 15, 17; the fence commits under `active_config FOR UPDATE`). `ErrorCode` gains `delete_everything_in_progress` (EN/DE text).
  - `api/edge.py`: `def fence_exempt(method: str, path: str) -> bool`, on `scope["path"]` before routing (`route_label` reads `scope["route"]`, which only the router sets, `api/edge.py:86`, so no route template is known at this point): `POST /api/auth/login`, `POST /api/auth/logout`, a `POST` whose path matches `^/api/system/purges/[0-9]+/retry$` (the only recovery of a failed delete-everything, whose global fence stays) and every path under `/api/privacy/`. Any other state-changing `/api` request, after the CSRF check: while `global_fence_active` → 409 `delete_everything_in_progress` before the app sees it and before its body is read (one read on the edge's engine); otherwise the edge sets `fence_guard.WRITE_GUARD` for the rest of the request, its token reset in a `finally` (a test's `ASGITransport` runs the app in the test's own task, so a leaked value would guard the test's later transactions), and calls the app (P3, D15, Deviation 15).
  - `db/fence_guard.py` *(new; in `db/` so that `db/snapshot.py` can use it without importing the api)*: `WRITE_GUARD: Final[ContextVar[bool]]` (default False); `def install_fence_guard(engine: AsyncEngine) -> None` (idempotent: `event.contains` first) registers a `begin` listener on `engine.sync_engine` (SQLAlchemy dispatches it inside `Connection._begin_impl`, which allows a statement there): while `WRITE_GUARD` is set, the transaction's first statement is `db/guards`' global-fence read (`conn.exec_driver_sql`), and an active fence raises `Refusal(409, "delete_everything_in_progress")` before anything is written (the pool's reset rolls the read back). It closes the window between the edge's read and a request's own transactions for every write of a guarded request, those that take no `active_config` included (the CV upload and paste): a transaction that began before the fence committed is one the quiesce waits for (`xact_start < since`, Task 9), and one that begins after reads the committed fence. SQLAlchemy runs the listener in a greenlet that shares the request's context (`sqlalchemy/util/concurrency.py:99`, SQLAlchemy 2.1.4), so it sees the variable. `@contextmanager def suspended() -> Iterator[None]` clears it, around `persist_unhandled_alert` (a 500's alert is written even under the fence) and around `db/snapshot.read_snapshot`'s transaction. A read snapshot cannot carry the guard: its `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY` must be the transaction's first statement (`db/snapshot.py:19-27`), PostgreSQL refuses an isolation change after the guard's read, and a read-only transaction refuses every write anyway (SQLSTATE 25006). Without the suspension the group split and score-individually, which commit their change and then answer from a fresh snapshot (`api/routes/groups.py:129-133, 169, 196`), would answer 500 after their write committed. `POST /api/settings/dry-run`'s `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY` (`api/routes/settings.py:481-482`) stays valid after the guard's read: PostgreSQL refuses only a later isolation change or a return to read-write. `create_app` installs the guard on the app's engine, the lifespan's or an injected one. No api route re-checks the global fence itself (`save_settings`, the profile confirm and activation and `activate_shipped_version` included): the guard covers every route the edge does not exempt.
  - `claim_operation`: under an active `global` fence a non-`purge` operation is not claimed: `not_before := now + GLOBAL_FENCE_RETRY` (`timedelta(minutes=1)`), `attempts` unchanged, returns None. So a replay or apply waiting out delete-everything spends no attempt (M4 Ruling T12-b's drain ends here).
  - `async def wait_operation(conn, claim: OperationClaim, *, error_code: str, not_before: datetime) -> None` and `OperationContext.wait_in(conn, *, error_code, not_before)`: the claimed operation back to `pending` with `not_before` and `error_code`, `attempts` unchanged, never `failed`, `FencedOut` when the claim is lost. It is the handler-side form of the claim rule above, for a wait on a condition that has its own end (a purge in progress, a running eval run): `requeue_in` spends one of the ten attempts (`MAX_OPERATION_ATTEMPTS`, `worker/operations.py:121, 549`), so a requeue every minute would fail an operation that is only waiting after ten minutes. Tasks 12 and 17 use it.
  - The writers of P2, each checking `is_fenced` after taking the job row `FOR SHARE`, in the transaction that writes (§20.2's consumer table gains them, Deviation 14). A job row found gone (a purge deleted it after the writer read its work) is treated as fenced: the writer writes nothing, so a late writer never re-creates a row for a deleted job, nor an `embeddings` row for a deleted revision's text:
    - `det_completeness` and the scope-apply precompute skip a fenced job;
    - `record_feedback` and `set_flags` (activating a case) refuse a fenced job with `Refusal(409, "conflict_state", detail="purge_in_progress")` (M4 Ruling P3's mapping); Task 14 adds `set_flags`' `active_config FOR SHARE` and its refusal of a purged or profile-fenced version;
    - group-wide user actions refuse when a member is fenced, and `split_member` refuses a fenced member (same refusal);
    - `awaiting_embedding`, the `embed` node's write and the similarity fill skip revisions of fenced jobs;
    - `score_now`, `retry` and `score_individually` answer 404 for a fenced job, as the detail does;
    - the jobs list omits fenced jobs (D21: `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM purge_fences WHERE released_at IS NULL) OR NOT is_fenced(j.id)`).
  - The batch-item settlement's gap is Task 11's.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ids.uuid7`; the tables of Task 1; `transition()`; `raise_alert`/`resolve_alert`; `create_operation` (`worker/operations.py:381`); `reconciler(name, *cadences)` (`worker/reconcilers.py:99`).
- Produces:
  - Types (`privacy/manifests.py`):

    ```python
    ManifestKind = Literal["delete_everything", "source_purge", "profile_purge", "retention_purge", "job_tombstone", "golden_case_delete"]
    FenceKind = Literal["global", "source", "profile", "jobs"]
    class Bound(TypedDict): by: Literal["id", "parent", "created_at", "thread"]; table: NotRequired[str]; column: NotRequired[str]
    class FenceOp(TypedDict): op: Literal["fence"]; kind: FenceKind; key: str; job_ids: list[int]
    class RowDeleteOp(TypedDict): op: Literal["row_delete"]; table: str; ids: NotRequired[list[int]]; selector: NotRequired[str]; keep: NotRequired[list[int]]; bound: Bound
    class FieldRedactOp(TypedDict): op: Literal["field_redact"]; table: str; ids: list[int]; columns: list[str]; marker: NotRequired[dict[str, str]]
    class FileRemoveOp(TypedDict): op: Literal["file_remove"]; volume: Literal["cv_store", "exports"]; keys: list[str]
    Operation = FenceOp | RowDeleteOp | FieldRedactOp | FileRemoveOp
    class Affected(TypedDict): job_ids: list[int]; profile_version_ids: list[int]; tuple_ids: list[int]
    class Watermark(TypedDict): fenced_at: str; max_ids: dict[str, int]
    class ManifestContent(TypedDict): format: Literal[1]; manifest_uuid: str; kind: ManifestKind; admitted_at: str; fenced_at: str; max_ids: dict[str, int]; operations: list[Operation]; affected: NotRequired[Affected]; watermark: NotRequired[Watermark]; retained: NotRequired[dict[str, list[int]]]
    ```

    Selectors: `all`, `source:<id>`.

    **Every postcondition must stay true under later normal activity.** §19.5 (L6268) replays a `completed` or `shipped` manifest whenever a postcondition fails. So a check that later activity could break would make a later `migrate` re-run the purge (for delete-everything, its settings reset). Two rules follow:
    - A selector names only a property the row is born with and never loses: `all`, or `source:<id>` on `postings` and `fetch_requests` (their source) and on `ad_keys` (its `key_ns`), all fixed at insertion. It is evaluated only over rows within the bound.
    - Any membership that depends on a mutable column or on other rows is frozen at A2 as an explicit `ids` list: open or resolved, `doing` or finished, active or inactive, referenced or not, the rows a kind keeps. Execution and every later check use that same list. A row named by id never comes back, because identities only advance (Task 23).
    - A state a kind sets on a kept row is checked relative to the bounds (for example "a pointer NULL or above `max_ids`"), never as an exact value a later save may change. `bound.by = "id"`: the table's identity column (`bound.column`, default `id`; `generation` for `login_lockouts`, and the new surrogate `id` of `api_idempotency`, both Task 1) `≤ max_ids[table]`; `"parent"`: a table without an identity column, bounded by its parent's id column (`bound.table`, `bound.column`) against `max_ids[bound.table]`; `"created_at"`: `created_at ≤ fenced_at` (D3); `"thread"`, for the checkpoint tables (`checkpoints`, `checkpoint_writes`, `checkpoint_blobs`: text keys, no identity, no `created_at`): the row's `thread_id` names its owner, and the owner's id is within that owner table's bound. The thread forms are `run-<run>-a<n>` (`worker/runs.py:137-139`), a cycle's `<prefix><cycle>` (`graphs/score/graph.py:112-114`), and a profile execution's `<prefix>-<subject>-<kind><operation>-a<n>` (`graphs/profile/graph.py:137-146`). A thread of any other form is a postcondition violation, never a silent pass. `fenced_at` is every manifest's A0 commit cutoff, the `fence_committed_at` read after A0 committed (the watermark's `fenced_at` for a source or profile purge), so the `created_at` bound also serves delete-everything (sessions, idempotency records). `admitted_at` is A0's own clock value (the `now` of `open_purge`), written in A0's transaction as `deletion_manifests.created_at` and as every audit value A0 writes (a source purge's `sources.purge_fence_at` and its disabling settings version's `created_at`); so `admitted_at ≤ fenced_at`, and the two differ by the commit and the read. A check of A0's own writes (a postcondition, a replay's generation rule) compares with `admitted_at`; a bound on rows that other transactions wrote compares with `fenced_at`. Cascade-only tables are not listed; their postcondition follows their parent's. A fence op's `key` is the source id, the profile version id, `jobs` or `global`.
  - `MANIFEST_FORMAT: Final = 1`; `def canonical_bytes(content: ManifestContent) -> bytes` (`json.dumps(sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)` in UTF-8; D2); `def parse_manifest(data: bytes, *, file_name: str) -> ManifestContent`, raising `ManifestInvalid(reason)` for a non-JSON file, an unknown `format` or op, or a file name other than `<manifest_uuid>.json`.
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class ManifestRow: id: int; manifest_uuid: UUID; kind: ManifestKind; status: Literal["executing", "completed", "shipped"]; request: Mapping[str, JSONValue]; file_sha256: bytes | None; scope: ManifestContent | None; created_at: datetime; completed_at: datetime | None; shipped_at: datetime | None`.
  - `async def insert_manifest(conn, *, kind: ManifestKind, request: Mapping[str, JSONValue], now: datetime) -> ManifestRow` (`executing`, a uuid7, the request, empty scope, `created_at = now`: the manifest's `admitted_at`); `async def max_identity_ids(conn) -> dict[str, int]` (per table, the maximum of its identity column of the `public` schema, or 0; one identity column per table); `def write_manifest_file(directory: Path, content: ManifestContent) -> bytes` (the sha256; `<uuid>.json.tmp` → fsync → rename → fsync of the directory; an existing file with equal bytes returns its hash; other bytes raise `ManifestConflict`: never overwritten); `async def record_manifest(conn, manifest: ManifestRow, content: ManifestContent, sha256: bytes) -> None` (A3: `file_sha256`, `scope := content`, and `affected.job_ids` appended to the manifest's `source` fence); `async def complete_manifest(conn, manifest_id: int) -> None` (`executing → completed`).
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Violation: op_index: int; op: str; table: str | None; detail: str` (ids and counts only); `@dataclass(frozen=True) class FileRoots: cv_store: Path; exports: Path`; `async def check_postconditions(conn, content: ManifestContent, *, files: FileRoots) -> list[Violation]`, §19.5's column: `row_delete` — no named row, or no row within the bound that matches the (immutable) selector, apart from the kept rows the content lists by id; `field_redact` — every surviving named row has the columns NULL and the marker set (for `jobs.current_revision_id`: it names none of the revisions the manifest deletes; for `exports`, any content-free state of Task 17's table, `purged`, `expired` or `failed`, satisfies the marker); `file_remove` — no named file; `fence` — no active fence of the manifest (a final-state condition: the completing transaction releases the fences first, then checks, and rolls back on a violation, Tasks 12 and 25), and for the stored affected jobs that still exist, no `field_provenance` entry, current-revision lineage row, current `det_results` instance or golden case at or below the watermark draws on the purged source. For every manifest that deletes `llm_calls` rows, also: no `llm_spend_ledger` request entry is `reserved` or `dispatched` without its call (G04; Task 11's `settle_for_purge`), since no reader could settle it any more.
  - `privacy/fences.py` gains: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class FenceSpec: kind: FenceKind; source_id: str | None = None; profile_version_id: int | None = None; job_ids: tuple[int, ...] = ()`; `async def insert_fence(conn, *, manifest_id: int, spec: FenceSpec) -> int`; `async def release_fences(conn, manifest_id: int) -> int`; `QUIESCE_POLL_S: Final = 1.0`, `QUIESCE_TIMEOUT: Final = timedelta(minutes=10)`; `async def fence_committed_at(engine) -> datetime` (`clock_timestamp()` read in a new statement after the fence transaction committed); `async def wait_for_quiesce(engine, *, since: datetime, clock, sleep) -> bool` (D1: polls outside any transaction `SELECT 1 FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE usename = 'jobhunter_app' AND pid <> pg_backend_pid() AND xact_start < :since`; False on timeout).
  - `AppConfig.manifests_dir: Path` (`JOBHUNTER_MANIFESTS_DIR`, default `/data/manifests`) and `AppConfig.exports_dir: Path` (`JOBHUNTER_EXPORTS_DIR`, default `/data/exports`); the app image's runtime stage creates both `10001:10001` 0700 (R18); `compose.dev.yaml` adds the volumes (worker both rw, api `exports:ro`, migrate `manifests`, `cv_store` and `exports`, the last because a replayed or pending-retry purge removes export archives, Task 25).
  - Reconciler `manifest_shipping` (tick; Deviation 43): a `completed` manifest named by a shipment item with an equal `file_sha256` → `shipped` with the earliest such shipment; a `completed` one older than 1 h without one → `privacy.manifest_unshipped` (critical, subject `manifest:<uuid>`), resolved when shipped.
  - Reconciler `purge_fences` (heavy; D7), for each `executing` manifest: its newest purge operation `failed`, or `running` with `heartbeat_at` older than 1 h → `privacy.purge_failed` (critical, subject `manifest:<id>`), the fence kept and nothing re-queued; no `pending`, `running` or `failed` purge operation → a new one (`create_operation(kind="purge", payload={"manifest_id": id}, dedup_key=f"purge:{uuid}")`) and the alert; resolved when the manifest leaves `executing`.
  - The `operations` retention rule keeps every purge operation whose manifest is `executing` (D7).

  - `DbRole = Literal["app", "migrator", "backup", "restore"]`; `AppConfig.dsn(driver, *, db_role: DbRole | None = None, database: str | None = None) -> str` (default: api and worker → app, migrate and restore → migrator, backup → backup); `@dataclass(frozen=True) class ResticSettings: repository: str; region: str | None; cache_dir: Path`; `AppConfig.restic: ResticSettings | None` from `RESTIC_REPOSITORY`, `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION`, `RESTIC_CACHE_DIR` (default `<staging_dir>/.restic-cache`); `AppConfig.staging_dir: Path` (`JOBHUNTER_STAGING_DIR`, default `/data/staging`), which Tasks 24 and 26 use for every staging path instead of the literal, so their in-process tests can point it at a temporary directory; the loaded names gain the prefix `RESTIC_` and `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION`.
  - Two named source stages near the top of the `Dockerfile`, because BuildKit does not expand a variable in `COPY --from` (the reason the `uv` stage exists, `Dockerfile:9`): `ARG RESTIC_IMAGE`, `ARG PGVECTOR_IMAGE`, `FROM ${RESTIC_IMAGE} AS restic`, `FROM ${PGVECTOR_IMAGE} AS pgclient`. The `backup` stage follows `runtime` (`FROM runtime AS backup`; root for its copies, then `USER 10001:10001`; Deviation 29): `COPY --from=restic /usr/bin/restic /usr/local/bin/restic`; `COPY --from=pgclient` of `/usr/lib/postgresql/17/bin/{pg_dump,pg_restore,psql}` to `/opt/pgclient/bin/` and of the nine libraries the runtime lacks (`libpq.so.5`, `libgssapi_krb5.so.2`, `libldap-2.5.so.0`, `libkrb5.so.3`, `libk5crypto.so.3`, `libkrb5support.so.0`, `liblber-2.5.so.0`, `libsasl2.so.2`, `libkeyutils.so.1`, with their versioned targets) from `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/` to `/opt/pgclient/lib/`, registered in `/etc/ld.so.conf.d/pgclient.conf` with `ldconfig`; `/data/staging` and `/data/restic-repo` (the dev shipper's local repository) created `10001:10001` 0700 before `USER 10001:10001`: a fresh named volume takes the owner and mode of its mount point, so without it the `restic_repo` volume would start `root`-owned and `restic init` as 10001 would fail. The compose files pass `RESTIC_IMAGE` and `PGVECTOR_IMAGE` from the lock as build args.
  - `backup` is now the `Dockerfile`'s last stage, so a build without a target would produce it: every build of the app image names `target: runtime` — `compose.dev.yaml`'s `x-app-build` (here), `compose.test.yaml` (Task 21), the `images` and release jobs (Task 29).

### Task 11: Target-set deletion for purges: the in-batch fix (P1)

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/deletion_graph.py`, `tests/integration/test_purge_in_batch.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/batches.py` (`record_cancel`, `abandon_preparing`), `scoring/batch_operations.py` (its reconciler uses `abandon_preparing`), `scoring/batch_results.py` (purged lines), `scoring/batch_adoption.py` (the membership it matches), `scoring/batch_items.py` (the fence decision before the settlement stores output), `scoring/targets.py` (`delete_target_set`'s `on_cancelled` hook), `llm/spend_ledger.py` (`lock_for_purge`, `settle_for_purge`; a purged call's late settlement), `tests/integration/test_batch_decisions.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `delete_target_set`, `cancel_targets`, `cancel_batched`, `TARGET_SET_CHUNK` (`scoring/targets.py:1263, 1291, 1302, 1330`); `request_cancel` and its `_RECORD` statement (`scoring/batches.py:447, 477`); the provider cancel sent after commit by `batch_results.cancel_requested` and the poll (`scoring/batch_results.py:202`); the `custom_id` form `t<target>_a<attempt>` (`scoring/batches.py:553`); `process_results` (`scoring/batch_results.py:523`); `process_item`, `_settle`, `_fenced` (`scoring/batch_items.py:300, 355, 417`); the adoption's membership `ours = items_of(...)` (`scoring/batch_adoption.py:298-313`, equal / partial / disjoint against the provider's custom ids); the release (`scoring/batches.py:1330-1350`: entries `ambiguous`, holds released, targets moved out of `in_batch`, attempts `released`), after which the poll still reads a released batch with a provider id (`_POLLED`, `scoring/batch_results.py:140-144`) and the adoption a released one without (`scoring/batch_adoption.py:82`); `purged_batch_items` (Task 1); the spend ledger's `dispatched → ambiguous` settlement as §20.4 maps it; the online entries' readers, each an inner join from the ledger row to its `llm_calls` row (`_ENTRY` `llm/spend_ledger.py:583`, which `settle`, `release_never_dispatched` and `mark_dispatched` read; `_UNSETTLED` `:1881` and `_recover` `:1939` of `ledger_unsettled`), `_release` and `_mark_ambiguous` (`:1230, 1924`), `lock_month`; `mark_dispatched`'s owner re-check under the owner lock (`:1160-1166`); the attempt calls `delete_target_set` deletes (`scoring/targets.py:1324`).
- Produces:
  - `scoring/batches.py`: `async def record_cancel(conn, *, submission_ids: Sequence[int], now: datetime) -> list[int]` (the per-submission form of `_RECORD` over `IN_FLIGHT`); `async def abandon_preparing(conn, submission_id: int, *, now: datetime) -> int` (the `preparing → abandoned` path of the `batch_submissions` reconciler, factored out: its entries `reserved → not_billed(never_dispatched)`, its targets back to `pending`, their attempts `cancelled('batch_abandoned')`; the targets moved).
  - `llm/spend_ledger.py`: `async def lock_for_purge(conn, ledger_ids: Sequence[int], *, now: datetime) -> dict[date, BudgetRow]` (the entries' month rows in ascending order, then their funded entries' bulk rows in id order: schedule steps 6 and 7); `@dataclass(frozen=True) class PurgeSettled: released: int; ambiguous: int`; `async def settle_for_purge(conn, ledger_ids: Sequence[int], rows: Mapping[date, BudgetRow], *, now: datetime) -> PurgeSettled` (the ledger rows in id order: a `reserved` online entry → `not_billed('never_dispatched')` through `_release`, the counters `-r` and a funded allocation credited; a `dispatched` online entry → `ambiguous` through `_mark_ambiguous`, counted at its reservation as `ledger_unsettled` would count it; any other state untouched). Every purge that deletes an `llm_calls` row whose entry is `reserved` or `dispatched` settles it with these two first, in the same transaction: every later reader of an online entry joins its call, so an entry left unsettled when its call goes could never be settled or recovered (G04). `settle` and `release_never_dispatched` of an entry whose call a purge deleted find the ledger row alone, already settled by the purge: a no-op logged `llm_settle_call_purged`, never a `LookupError` (a purged target's late online response, or its dispatch marker fenced by the purge).
  - `scoring/targets.py`: `delete_target_set(conn, target_ids, *, now, on_cancelled: Callable[[AsyncConnection], Awaitable[None]] | None = None)`, the hook run after step 2 (the targets cancelled) and before step 3's deletes.
  - `privacy/deletion_graph.py`: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class PurgedTargets: targets: int; submissions_cancel_requested: tuple[int, ...]; submissions_abandoned: tuple[int, ...]; entries_ambiguous: int; online_entries_released: int; online_entries_ambiguous: int`; `async def purge_target_set(conn, target_ids: Sequence[int], *, manifest_id: int, now: datetime) -> PurgedTargets` (at most `TARGET_SET_CHUNK`; the caller holds the job rows `FOR UPDATE` in id order). For every submission holding an `in_batch` target of the set: `preparing` → `abandon_preparing`; `submit_started`, `submit_ambiguous`, `submitted`, `ended` → `record_cancel` and the set's items' spend entries `dispatched → ambiguous` (counted at their reservation until the month closes). Independently of the targets' states, one `purged_batch_items (submission_id, custom_id, manifest_id)` row (ids only) for every batch item of the set about to be deleted whose submission can still return results: `submit_started`, `submit_ambiguous`, `submitted`, `ended` or `released`. A release moves the targets out of `in_batch` and settles their entries, yet its batch is still polled or adopted, and a target cancelled individually (§10.10) leaves its item in a running batch; without the row their late lines would be foreign again. These rows are the positive evidence the readers below need. Then the online entries: the set's attempt calls whose entries are `reserved` or `dispatched` (not batch) are read, `lock_for_purge` takes their month and bulk rows before `delete_target_set` takes the targets (steps 6 and 7 before step 10), and `delete_target_set`'s `on_cancelled` calls `settle_for_purge` once step 1 has cancelled the targets (a cancelled target's coroutine can no longer dispatch: `mark_dispatched` re-checks its owner under the owner lock). `delete_target_set`'s step 1 cancels each target individually (`batch_submission_id := NULL`) and whose step 7 deletes the items and calls; the ledger rows stay. The batch's other targets stay `in_batch`; their items come back `canceled` and retry online through the existing `batch_canceled` path (P1, M5-R3, Deviation 13).
  - `batch_results`: a line with no item of the submission is `purged` only when a `purged_batch_items` row names `(submission_id, custom_id)`: skipped, nothing stored, never `foreign`, no anomaly; `_Streamed` gains `purged: int`; the completion counts purged lines as accounted for, so the submission reaches `reconciled`. Any other line without an item stays `foreign` (the existing `t999999_a9` case of `tests/integration/test_batch_poll.py:438` keeps its anomaly).
  - `batch_adoption`: the submission's membership is its items' custom ids plus its `purged_batch_items` custom ids, so a provider batch whose ids equal the original set is still adopted after a purge deleted some of its items (rule 1 `equal`, not `partial`).
  - `batch_items.process_item`: the fence decision runs before `_settle` stores anything; the item of a fenced or cancelled target settles its spend from usage and stores no `output_json` (P2).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** (`test_purge_in_batch.py`, the fake Batches API and an injected clock). `test_a_purged_in_batch_target_no_longer_blocks_the_batch` reproduces the M3 stuck batch first, then pins the fix: a `submitted` submission of two targets, one of them purged; on the batch's end, the submission reaches `reconciled`, the purged line counts `purged`, no `batch.custom_id_anomaly` opens, the purged item's spend entry is `ambiguous`, and a new submission can start (`batch_one_in_flight` free). On the current code this test fails with the anomaly and the submission stuck `ended` (step 2 shows it). Also: `test_the_submission_cancel_is_recorded_and_sent_after_commit` (`cancel_requested_at` set; the next poll calls `messages.batches.cancel` once while in progress); `test_the_other_target_stays_in_batch_and_retries_online_when_canceled`; `test_a_preparing_submission_is_abandoned_and_never_sent`; `test_a_submit_ambiguous_submission_found_later_settles_without_output`; `test_an_ambiguous_submission_with_a_purged_item_is_still_adopted` (the provider's custom ids equal the original set; adoption treats it as equal, then the purged line counts `purged`); `test_a_fenced_item_stores_no_output_json`; `test_a_released_lost_batch_with_a_purged_item_reconciles_on_late_retrieval` (a lost `submitted` submission released, then one of its targets purged; the poll observes the released batch, which ends: the purged line counts `purged`, no anomaly opens, the submission reaches `reconciled`); `test_a_released_ambiguous_submission_with_a_purged_item_is_still_adopted` (`submit_ambiguous`, released, then purged: the provider's ids equal the original set, rule 1 `equal`); `test_a_target_cancelled_out_of_a_running_batch_leaves_evidence_when_purged` (a supersession cancelled it individually, its submission stays `submitted`; its line counts `purged`); `test_an_in_progress_targets_reserved_entry_is_released_before_its_call_goes` (`not_billed('never_dispatched')`, the month's counters and the funded allocation `-r`; the coroutine's dispatch marker is then fenced and its `release_never_dispatched` a no-op); `test_a_dispatched_online_entry_ends_ambiguous_and_its_late_response_is_a_no_op` (the coroutine's `settle` after the purge logs `llm_settle_call_purged`, raises nothing, and leaves the entry `ambiguous`); `test_ledger_unsettled_finds_nothing_stranded_after_a_purge` (no `reserved` or `dispatched` entry without its call); `test_a_foreign_line_still_raises_the_anomaly` (a line `t999999_a9` with no purge evidence, and an unparsable one).

### Task 12: The purge protocol and the golden-case deletion with its manifest

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/purge.py`, `privacy/kinds/__init__.py`, `privacy/kinds/golden_case.py`, `tests/integration/test_purge_protocol.py`, `tests/crash/test_c31_manifest_fsync_crash.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/worker/operations.py` (`OPERATION_LOCKS["purge"]`; `OperationDeps.deadman`), `worker/main.py` (`_REGISTERING_MODULES` gains `jobhunter.privacy.purge` and `jobhunter.privacy.kinds`), `privacy/deletion_graph.py` (the export ops), `scoring/golden.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 9's manifest and fence functions (`insert_manifest`, `write_manifest_file`, `record_manifest`, `complete_manifest`, `check_postconditions`, `FenceSpec`, `insert_fence`, `release_fences`, `fence_committed_at`, `wait_for_quiesce`); `register_operation_handler`, `create_operation`, `OperationContext.finish_in`/`requeue_in`, `lock_operation_owner` (`worker/operations.py:199-244, 381, 465`); `fence_epoch`; `afault_point`; `CvStore`; `cfg.exports_dir`; `golden.delete_case` (`scoring/golden.py:460`).
- Produces:
  - `PURGE_DEDUP_PREFIX: Final = "purge:"`; the payload `{"manifest_id": int}` only: the request lives on the manifest row (`deletion_manifests.request`, Task 1), so an operation the `purge_fences` reconciler or the retry route creates, a pending retry that replay completes, and a manifest whose operation delete-everything cancelled and deleted all compute the same content from the same request (Deviation 53); `OPERATION_LOCKS["purge"] = "purge"`: one purge at a time, so execution follows the UUIDv7 stream order (D5).
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class OpenedPurge: manifest_id: int; manifest_uuid: UUID; operation_id: int`; `async def open_purge(conn, *, kind: ManifestKind, request: Mapping[str, JSONValue], fences: Sequence[FenceSpec], now: datetime, defer: Deferrer | None) -> OpenedPurge`: phase A0 in the caller's transaction, at `admitted_at = now`, which already holds `active_config FOR UPDATE` (and, on an api route, its idempotency row): the manifest row with its request (`insert_manifest(request=request, now=now)`), its fence rows and the purge operation (payload `{manifest_id}`).
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class LiveEffects: batches_client: BatchesClient; deadman: DeadmanPinger | None`: the purge's external effects, which only a live purge has. `run_purge` builds it from the worker's `OperationDeps`: its `batches_client` (`worker/operations.py:178`, the process's Message Batches client) and `deadman`, a new field holding `WorkerRuntime.deadman` (Task 5), `None` without a pinger. Replay (Task 25) passes `None`: it builds no Batches client and no pinger, and needs no Anthropic or healthchecks secret, so the migrate, restore and backup roles keep the secrets they have (Deviation 56).
  - `class PurgeRun`: `engine`, `cfg`, `now: datetime`, `cv_store: CvStore`, `mode: Literal["live", "replay"]`, `effects: LiveEffects | None` (set exactly when `mode` is `live`), `async def chunk(self, *, config: Literal["share", "update"] = "share", job_ids: Sequence[int] = (), with_groups_of: Sequence[int] = ()) -> AsyncContextManager[Chunk]` (`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Chunk: conn: AsyncConnection; held: frozenset[int]`): a transaction that takes its locks in §3.3.3's order (L314) — (1) `fence_epoch` (live only), (2) `active_config` `FOR SHARE` (or `FOR UPDATE` for a step that re-points it, delete-everything's step 8), (4) the `job_ids` rows and those of every duplicate-group co-member of `with_groups_of`, `FOR UPDATE` in one ascending-id statement (`merge.lock_jobs(conn, job_ids, with_groups_of=with_groups_of)`, `dedup/merge.py:292`), then the co-members read again (`merge.co_members`, `:306`): a member that joined between the statement's snapshot and its lock rolls the transaction back and the chunk starts again, at most `CHUNK_LOCK_ROUNDS: Final = 3` rounds (as `groups._LOCK_ROUNDS`), then `CoMemberChanged`, which `run_purge` requeues (`co_member_changed`, 1 min); `held` is the locked set, (5) `lock_operation_owner` (live only) — before the kind's statements, which then follow the schedule's later steps (bulk rows, the month row, ledger rows; `scoring/targets.py:166-178`), with `ranking.bump` the transaction's last statement. Replay (Task 25) runs the same order as the migrator, without the epoch and the owner. No kind takes a lock before `chunk` returns.
  - `class KindProcedure(Protocol)`: `kind: ManifestKind`; `deletes_exports: bool`; `async def compute(self, conn, manifest: ManifestRow, *, now: datetime) -> ManifestContent` (the request is `manifest.request`) (A2, after the quiesce, under the fence); `async def execute(self, run: PurgeRun, content: ManifestContent) -> None` (phase B in idempotent chunks: job rows `FOR UPDATE` in id order before the rows to delete are selected, every statement bounded by `max_ids` (D16); a `file_remove` runs only after the transaction that changed the rows naming its files has committed, so no committed row that still claims a file (a `ready` export, a retained CV) ever names a missing one, and a crash in between leaves only a file no row names, which the idempotent re-execution removes); `async def postconditions(self, conn, content: ManifestContent) -> list[Violation]` (the kind's own); `async def reapply_admission(self, conn, manifest: ManifestRow, *, now: datetime) -> None` (replay's re-application of A0's effects beyond the manifest, its fences and its operation; a no-op for every kind but the source purge, Task 15). `KINDS: dict[ManifestKind, KindProcedure]`, `def register_kind(proc: KindProcedure) -> KindProcedure`.
  - Handler `run_purge(ctx: OperationContext) -> None` for kind `purge`, with a failure hook that raises `privacy.purge_failed` at once (Task 9's reconciler is the backstop):
    1. manifest not `executing` → `succeeded`;
    2. no `file_sha256`: when `<manifest_uuid>.json` already exists in `cfg.manifests_dir` (a crash after the file's rename, before A3), it is adopted, never recomputed: `parse_manifest` must succeed with the row's uuid, kind and `format` 1 (else `failed('manifest_invalid')`, fence kept), then `record_manifest` with the file's own content and the sha256 of its bytes — nothing was deleted before A3 and the fence held, so the file's content is still the purge's; otherwise `wait_for_quiesce(since=fence_committed_at)` (timeout → `ctx.requeue_in(error_code="quiesce_timeout", not_before=now + 1 min)`), `compute`, `write_manifest_file`, `record_manifest` (A1–A3);
    3. phase B: `execute` on the stored `scope` (never re-derived), then for a kind with `deletes_exports` the export ops;
    4. phase C, one transaction: `release_fences`, then the typed postconditions (the fence op's "no active fence" now holds) and the kind's; a violation rolls the transaction back, so the fences stay active, and a second transaction ends the operation `failed('postcondition')`; else `complete_manifest`, resolve `privacy.purge_failed`, `ctx.finish_in(status="succeeded")` in the same transaction.
    Fault points (test env only): `privacy.purge.before_file`, `privacy.purge.after_file`, `privacy.purge.after_record`, `privacy.purge.mid_delete`, `privacy.purge.before_complete`.
  - `privacy/deletion_graph.py`: `async def export_ops(conn, *, max_export_id: int) -> list[Operation]` (A2: the `running` and `ready` exports within the bound → `field_redact exports storage_path` with marker `status = 'purged'`, and `file_remove exports <id>-*` per export; executed by Task 17's table of export states: a named row still `ready` or `running` → `purged`, a row already content-free left as it is); `async def purge_exports(run: PurgeRun, ops: Sequence[Operation]) -> int` (the rows `purged` first, the files unlinked after that commit, as `execute`'s file rule says). Every kind but `job_tombstone` has `deletes_exports = True` (M5-R3; Deviation 18).

**Interfaces:**
