- Produces:
  - `TOMBSTONE_AFTER: Final = timedelta(days=90)`, `TOMBSTONE_BATCH: Final = 200`, `TOMBSTONE_BATCHES_PER_RUN: Final = 10`.
  - Retention rule `job_tombstones` (after `score_history`): up to ten batches of up to 200 root jobs (unmerged, not synthetic) with `availability IN ('closed', 'stale')` and `availability_changed_at < now − 90 d`, not saved or applied, no active golden case on a revision of theirs or of a job merged into them; each batch opens one purge (`kind="job_tombstone"`, request `{"job_ids": [...]}`, a `jobs` fence naming the batch and every job merged into it) in a transaction holding `active_config FOR UPDATE` (D12; M3 plan Task 23's exemption contract).
  - `compute` re-checks the exemptions under the fence after the quiesce: a job that became saved, applied or golden-active since is left out of the content (its fence ends at completion). No `affected` set or watermark (not a source or profile purge). `deletes_exports = False` (M5-R3).
  - `execute`, per root job J and the set G of J and every job merged into it: first the targets, in chunks `chunk(job_ids=G)` of at most `TARGET_SET_CHUNK` (200) target ids each (selected `ORDER BY id LIMIT 200` after the lock, then `purge_target_set`; `delete_target_set` raises `ValueError` above 200), until none is left (the fence keeps new ones from being created); then one graph chunk `chunk(job_ids=G, with_groups_of=G)`, which also holds every co-member of their duplicate groups before the owner, and `deletion_graph.delete_job_graph(c.conn, J, write_tombstone=True, held=c.held, now=now)`, the ranking bump last. A restart in between resumes with the targets left (Deviation 50).
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class JobGraphDeleted: job_ids: tuple[int, ...]; revisions: int; postings: int; keys_reassigned: int; tombstone_id: int | None`; `deletion_graph.delete_job_graph(conn, job_id: int, *, write_tombstone: bool, held: Collection[int], now: datetime) -> JobGraphDeleted`: the one complete pre-deletion cleanup that tombstoning, the Adzuna purge (`write_tombstone=False`) and delete-everything (`write_tombstone=False`) share — §20.2's tombstoning steps 4–7, for J and, first and deepest first, every job whose `merged_into_job_id` leads to J (`jobs.merged_into_job_id` restricts deleting J); the caller holds those job rows and every co-member of their duplicate groups `FOR UPDATE` (`held`) and has deleted their targets (a target left raises `InvariantViolation`):
    - (4) the jobs' remaining golden cases deleted when inactive (an active one raises `InvariantViolation`: tombstoning excluded it, the Adzuna purge and delete-everything deleted every case that draws on the purged data first); before the revisions, because `golden_cases.job_revision_id` restricts;
    - (5) the `merge_events` with the job as `from_job_id` or `into_job_id` (both restrict, `0005_m1_collection.py:589-590`), and every `reversed_by_event_id` pointing at one of them cleared first; the `identity_conflicts` whose `posting_job_id` is the job deleted, and the job removed from other conflicts' `candidate_job_ids` (a conflict left with no candidate deleted); the job leaves its group through `groups.leave_group(conn, job, reason=groups.MEMBER_LEFT, held=held)`, which dissolves a group left with fewer than two members or re-elects one whose representative left, so `duplicate_groups.representative_job_id` (a restricting key) no longer names the job, and then settles the group's deferred targets (a survivor's target that is no longer deferrable moves to `pending`, D01); `representative.reelect` alone would neither check the locks nor settle (`dedup/representative.py:268`); `dedup.ambiguous_grouping` of the job resolved (M1 Carry-forward 11);
    - (6) `current_revision_id := NULL` (deferred), revisions and postings deleted (lineage, occurrences, `hard_filter_results`, observations and key sources cascade); a key that other jobs' postings also supply is re-assigned to the job of its earliest remaining provenance row, the job's other `ad_keys` deleted;
    - (7) with `write_tombstone`, a `job_tombstones` row whose `user_actions` holds the four timestamps `saved_at`, `applied_at`, `hidden_at`, `seen_at` as ISO-8601 with offset, never a note (M3 ledger T22-b), and `tombstone_keys` upserted `ON CONFLICT (key_hash) DO UPDATE SET tombstone_id = EXCLUDED.tombstone_id` (P15); then `user_job_actions` and the job deleted.
  - C10: `count_retained_current_revisions` needs no change, since a tombstoned job no longer exists; its comment says so.
  - `tests/support/privacy_seed.py`: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class SeedIds` (the ids each later test needs: a closed job with an inactive golden case, a saved job, a mixed-source job, an Adzuna-only job, a purgeable profile version, the live submission and the terminal ones …); `InFlight = Literal["preparing", "submit_started", "submit_ambiguous", "submitted", "ended"]`; `async def seed_two_year_old_graph(conn, *, now: datetime, live_submission: InFlight = "submitted") -> SeedIds`: rows in every table of the `public` schema dated about 730 days back. The seed is written against the real schema with every CHECK, unique index and trigger enabled, inserting each row directly in its state. Among the rows, targets in every state: the `in_batch` ones belong to the one live submission, in state `live_submission`, since `batch_one_in_flight` (`0008_m3_scoring.py:469-470`) allows one per database; the other submissions are terminal (`reconciled`, `abandoned`, and `released` with `released_at`, which its CHECK allows only in `released`, `ended` or `reconciled`, `:466-467`). A scenario that needs another live state runs on another seeded database (Task 16), results, det instances, golden cases active and inactive, feedback, groups, merge events, conflicts, tombstones, profile versions with tuples, vectors, proposals, CV files and drafts, settings and scope versions, runs, cycles, fetch requests with responses and items, alerts, exports, sessions, login attempts and lockouts, operations, idempotency rows. Tasks 14–16 and 25 use it. The seed rule, which every fixture of M5 follows too, is that rows satisfy the schema as it stands.
    - **Singletons and partial-unique indexes:** at most one live submission (`batch_one_in_flight`); one `running` export (`exports_one_running`, Task 1); one `queued` or `running` collect run (`runs_one_active_collect`); one `running` score cycle (`score_cycles_one_running`); one `active` bulk operation (`bulk_operations_one_active`); one open profile draft (`profile_drafts_one_open`); one current quota window per provider (`quota_windows_current`).
    - **Distinct keys:** open alerts by `dedup_key` (`alerts_open_dedup`); live operations by `(kind, dedup_key)` (`operations_live_dedup`); unpurged profile versions by `profile_hash` (`profile_versions_live_hash`); one current det instance per `(llm_result_id, scope_version_id)` (`det_results_current`); distinct `(kind, scheduled_slot)` runs (`runs_slot_uq`); golden cases by `(job_revision_id, profile_version_id)`; targets by `(job_revision_id, llm_tuple_id)`; live query texts (`queries_text_live`).
    - **Each row's CHECKs:** for example, a `confirmed` draft names its version and keeps `draft_json` unless `content_purged_at` is set; a `ready` proposal has `items`; a `submitted`, `ended` or `reconciled` submission has a `provider_batch_id`, and every one past `preparing` has `submit_started_at`; a `skipped_overlap` or `missed` run has `attempt_no = 0` and no `started_at`; a `manual` run has no `scheduled_slot`.
    - **Only the matrix's edges.** A fixture that moves a row afterwards does so through `transition()`, so the `assert_transition` trigger checks the edge.

### Task 14: The profile version purge

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/kinds/profile_purge.py`, `tests/integration/test_profile_purge.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/retention.py` (rule `profile_purges`), `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/golden.py` (`set_flags` takes `active_config FOR SHARE` and refuses a purged or fenced profile), `backend/src/jobhunter/llm/spend_ledger.py` (`settle` honours `content_purged_at`), `backend/src/jobhunter/api/routes/profile.py` (`request_query_proposal` takes `active_config FOR SHARE` first), `backend/src/jobhunter/queries/proposals.py` (`request_proposal`'s caller contract and its invariant check), `tests/integration/test_spend_ledger.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `open_purge`, `purge_target_set`, `CvStore.delete` (`profile/storage.py:99`); `cancel_operation` (`worker/operations.py:605`) and the owner check every reservation and dispatch marker makes, `lock_operation_owner` through `_lock_owner` (`llm/spend_ledger.py:288, 863, 1160`: the operation `running` under its claim, else `FencedOut`); `request_proposal` (`queries/proposals.py:327-345`: it locks the version row and checks only that it exists; a `running` proposal without a live operation gets a new one) and its callers: the confirm and activation of a version, under `active_config FOR UPDATE` (`profile/activation.py:6-8, 344`), and the proposal route, which read the active version without any lock before this task (`api/routes/profile.py:146, 457-461`); `profile_versions.purged_at`, `query_proposals.status = 'purged'`, `cv_files.purged_at`, `profile_drafts.content_purged_at` (`0006_m2_llm_profile_settings.py:86-159`); the activation's 409 `profile_purge_in_progress` (`profile/activation.py:142`); `retention.score_history_months`; `seed_two_year_old_graph` (Task 13).
- Produces:
  - Retention rule `profile_purges` (after `job_tombstones`): one purge per eligible version (D11): not the active version and not the previous one (the newest confirmed version before the active; §20.1 keeps the CV files of both), `purged_at IS NULL`, no target of its tuples non-terminal or ended within `retention.score_history_months`, no active golden case on it, no `score_feedback` naming it. A synthetic version (`profile_versions.synthetic_eval`) is never eligible, whatever its cases: the predicate is an unconditional `NOT synthetic_eval`, as Deviation 25 says. `seed-adversarial` reuses an unpurged synthetic version and re-creates active cases on it (`scoring/golden.py:574-576, 615-619, 647-649, 797-813`), so a synthetic version made eligible by deleting or deactivating its cases could be re-seeded after A0 and leave an active case on purged content (M3 ledger L535).
  - The version's eligibility (the rule above) is evaluated in A0's own transaction, after `active_config FOR UPDATE`, and **every writer that can make a version ineligible holds `active_config` too**, so no eligibility condition can change between A0's check and A2. Each condition and its writers:
    - active or previous: the confirm and activation, under `FOR UPDATE`;
    - a non-terminal or recent target of its tuples: the score lane and the user's target moves take `active_config FOR SHARE` first (`scoring/targets.py:71`) and serve the active tuple only;
    - feedback naming it, or a new or upserted golden case: `record_feedback` takes `active_config FOR SHARE` first and works on the active profile only (`scoring/golden.py:280-286`); the adversarial seed takes `FOR UPDATE` (`:778`);
    - **an active golden case on it: `set_flags`, which took no `active_config` lock** (`scoring/golden.py:494-516`, called by `api/routes/golden.py:215`). It now takes `active_config FOR SHARE` as its first statement, before the canary lock and the job row (§3.3.3 step 2). It then refuses to activate a case whose profile version is purged or under an unreleased `profile` or `global` fence, with `Refusal(409, "conflict_state", detail="purge_in_progress")`, read after that lock. If an activation commits first, A0's eligibility read sees the active case and admits nothing; if A0 commits first, the activation waits for it and then meets the fence, or after completion `purged_at`;
    - deleting a case or feedback only makes a version more eligible, so it needs no lock.
  - **Every proposal operation is created under `active_config`** (the one rule that makes "an operation for a purgeable version created after A0" impossible). `request_proposal`'s callers must hold `active_config` (`FOR SHARE` at least, the schedule's step 2) from before they read which version to propose for, and pass the active version or the one their transaction activates. The confirm and activation already hold it `FOR UPDATE`. `request_query_proposal` now takes `active_config FOR SHARE` before `run_idempotent` (with the fault point `profile.proposal.before_request` between its read of the active version and `request_proposal`, for the race test) (step 2 before the idempotency row and the domain rows, as `run_idempotent`'s contract asks) and reads the active version under it. Why this suffices:
    - A0 holds `active_config FOR UPDATE`, so a route that holds `FOR SHARE` either finishes first, and then A0's cancel, which runs after A0's lock, sees and cancels the operation it created; or it waits for A0, and then reads an active version that cannot be the fenced one, since activation of a fenced version answers 409.
    - No activation can move the active version while the route holds its `FOR SHARE`, so the version it read stays active until it commits.
    - `request_proposal` also raises `InvariantViolation` for a version that is purged or under an unreleased `profile` or `global` fence, read after its version lock. The rule makes this unreachable; it guards a future caller that forgets it.
  - A0, in one transaction under `active_config FOR UPDATE`: first every live `propose_queries` operation whose proposal is of the version is cancelled (`cancel_operation`; owner rows before domain rows, §9.1); then the `profile_versions` row and its `llm_tuples` rows are locked `FOR UPDATE`, so the eval's profile-fence check serialises on them (M3 ledger L535); then the manifest and a `profile` fence. Cancelling at admission, not in phase B, closes the call set for good. Every reservation and every dispatch marker of a proposal call takes its operation's row `FOR SHARE` and requires it `running` under its claim, so none can follow A0's commit. A reservation that committed before it is visible to A2, because the cancel waited for its `FOR SHARE`. No new proposal or extraction call of the version can start either: by the rule above every proposal operation is created under `active_config` for the active version or the one being activated, and the fenced version can be neither (activation answers 409 `profile_purge_in_progress`); the version's drafts are all `confirmed`. So the call set A2 computes is every call the version will ever have, and no call is chased after the fact (Deviation 54).
  - `compute`: `affected = {job_ids: [], profile_version_ids: [v], tuple_ids: [...]}`, the watermark, and the ops of §20.2's steps 2–6: the tuples' targets with their child rows; `row_delete profile_vectors`; `field_redact query_proposals items` (marker `status = 'purged'`) and `field_redact llm_calls output_json` (marker `content_purged_at`) over every call of the version, published or not: `purpose = 'propose_queries'` with `input_refs ->> 'profile_version_id'` = v (every attempt and logical call of every proposal, not only `query_proposals.llm_call_id`), and `purpose = 'extract_profile'` whose `input_refs ->> 'profile_draft_id'` names one of the version's drafts; `row_delete golden_cases ids=[…]` (the version's cases at A2, all inactive by eligibility, frozen as ids); `field_redact profile_versions profile_json` (marker `purged_at`); `field_redact cv_files raw_text_enc, stripped_text` (marker `purged_at`) with `file_remove cv_store <key>` when no retained version uses the file; `field_redact profile_drafts draft_json, reviewed_text` (marker `content_purged_at`); the export ops.
  - Settlement after the redaction: cancelling an operation (`worker/operations.py:605`) does not stop a provider call already sent, and its response settles in the gateway's own transaction (`llm/gateway.py:1362`), which writes the call's `output_json` (`spend_ledger.settle`, `llm/spend_ledger.py:1558, 1596`) whatever the operation's state. So `settle` now writes the call row through `transition_where` with `output_json` set by SQL, `CASE WHEN t.content_purged_at IS NULL THEN CAST(:output_json AS jsonb) END`: the expression is evaluated on the row the UPDATE locks, and PostgreSQL evaluates it again on the newest version after a lock wait, so a redaction that committed first wins whatever `settle` read before. The usage, cost and ledger state are settled as before (billing is kept); only the content is not stored. A redaction of a call still `reserved` or `dispatched` needs nothing else: its late response settles and stores nothing.
  - `execute` follows steps 2–6 (the version's operations were cancelled at A0): each proposal moves to `purged` through `transition` with `items := NULL` in the same update (Task 1's `running | ready | failed → purged` edges); the row, its tuples and their ids stay, so runs, cycles, submissions, bulk operations and ledgers keep valid references.

A request holds `active_config FOR SHARE`, has read the active version V and is paused before `request_proposal` at the route's fault point `profile.proposal.before_request` (`afault_point`, test env only). A0, one transaction under `active_config FOR UPDATE`: a new active settings version, the active one with `sources.enabled.adzuna = false`, through `save_settings(..., internal=True, now=now)` (D10); `sources.purge_fence_at := now`; the `queued` and `running` runs `cancelled('source_purge')`; the manifest (`insert_manifest(now=now)`) and a `source` fence for `adzuna`. All three audit values are this one `now`, the manifest's `admitted_at` (Task 9), not the later cutoff `fenced_at`. While a source purge is `executing`, another request is `Refusal(409, "conflict_state", detail="purge_in_progress")`.
  - Replay re-applies A0's durable effects, because `execute` is steps 3–5 only and a dump taken before the purge holds none of them: `KindProcedure.reapply_admission` (Task 12; a no-op for every other kind, whose A0 writes only the manifest, its fences and the operation), which Task 25's phase A calls under `active_config FOR UPDATE`. The source purge's re-applies three effects, each idempotent and each with a generation rule that keeps a later explicit choice: `sources.purge_fence_at := admitted_at` when it is NULL or earlier (never lowered); when the active settings version enables Adzuna and was created at or before the manifest's `admitted_at`, a new internal settings version disabling it (`save_settings(..., internal=True)`, as A0), while an active version created after `admitted_at` is the user's later choice and stays; the `queued` and `running` runs with `queued_at ≤ admitted_at` → `cancelled('source_purge')` (a run restored from the dump would otherwise resume collecting Adzuna after the fence's release).
  - `compute`, once and stored (the retry never re-derives it, §19.5 step 3): the tainted revisions (every revision with a `job_revision_sources` row for `adzuna`); the affected jobs (an Adzuna posting, a tainted revision, a `det_results` instance whose `det_inputs` name Adzuna, or a golden case on a tainted revision or with `'adzuna' = ANY(det_input_sources)`); the watermark; the ops: the targets of tainted revisions; `row_delete det_results` (untainted instances naming Adzuna); `row_delete golden_cases` (active or not; the agreement overrides the exemption); `row_delete llm_calls` (other eval calls whose `input_refs` name a tainted revision; their entries still `reserved` or `dispatched` settled first through `lock_for_purge` and `settle_for_purge`, Task 11); `row_delete postings selector source:adzuna`, the `adzuna` `ad_keys`, `row_delete fetch_requests selector source:adzuna`; `field_redact jobs current_revision_id` for the affected jobs whose current revision is tainted; `row_delete job_revisions` (the tainted ones); `row_delete embeddings ids=[…]`, the embeddings that at A2 only tainted revisions reference (D22), frozen there by Task 9's rule; the export ops; the `fence` op with the affected job ids.
  - `execute`, §20.5 steps 3–5: the derivatives (target-set deletion through `purge_target_set` in transactions of at most `TARGET_SET_CHUNK` targets, which cancels in-flight batches the P1 way; untainted det instances deleted and recomputed from the remaining data; golden cases with their results and calls; the other eval calls); the source data; then `async def rebuild_jobs(run: PurgeRun, job_ids: Sequence[int]) -> int` (the jobs rebuilt), one transaction per affected job that still exists (reuse a revision whose lineage names no Adzuna source, else `current_revision_id := NULL`, delete its tainted revisions, insert a new revision and point at it; recompute its current det instances for the active and pending scopes; Task 25's phase B calls it); jobs left with only Adzuna postings go through `delete_job_graph(write_tombstone=False)` as Task 13 runs it (their targets first, at most 200 a transaction, then a graph chunk holding their groups' co-members); finally the embeddings of deleted tainted revisions: every frozen id is deleted. A revision outside the deleted set that has reused one of them since A2 (identical text, `UNIQUE (embedding_model_id, text_sha256, input_kind)`) is first set back to `embedding_status = 'awaiting_embedding'` with `embedding_id` NULL in the same update (the CHECK allows only `awaiting_embedding` and `embedded`, with `embedded` exactly when `embedding_id` is set, `0005_m1_collection.py:519-532`), and `awaiting_embedding` gives it a new row above the bound. So the postcondition "no named row" holds for good: a check of "referenced only by tainted revisions" would flip when an unrelated revision with the same text is deleted later. No target is created; `missing_targets` creates them after the release.
  - Postconditions: Task 9's bounded `fence` checks over the stored affected jobs; the kind's own: `sources.purge_fence_at ≥ admitted_at` for `adzuna` (equal on the live path, where A0 wrote both), and the active settings version disables Adzuna unless it was created after `admitted_at` (only a new settings version re-enables it); data collected after a re-enablement lies above the watermark and is never touched.

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/kinds/delete_everything.py`, `backend/src/jobhunter/settings/reset.py`, `tests/integration/test_delete_everything.py`, `tests/integration/test_settings_reset.py`, `tests/crash/test_c26_delete_everything_crash.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/deletion_graph.py`, `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/batch_results.py` (`send_purge_cancel`, beside `cancel_requested`, whose poller behaviour is unchanged)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `open_purge`, `purge_target_set`, `delete_job_graph(write_tombstone=False)`; `cancel_operation` (`worker/operations.py:605`); `batches.abandon_preparing`, `record_cancel` (Task 11), `CANCEL_TRIES` and the poller's `cancel_requested`, left as it is (`scoring/batch_results.py:114, 199-219`); `PurgeRun.effects` (Task 12), live only: the worker's Batches client and `DeadmanPinger.ping_pending` (Task 5); `pause_reason`'s unacknowledged-alert gates (`scoring/cycles.py:257-265`) and the `submit_started | submit_ambiguous → released` transition; `seed.load_settings_v1()` (`db/seed.py:99`), `derived(settings, profile=None)` and `ensure_derived` (`settings/derive.py:72, 94`), the schedule reset of `save_settings` (`settings/save.py:156, 335`: `anchor_utc_for`, `latest_slot_at_or_before`); `ranking.bump`; `seed_two_year_old_graph`. Not `save_settings`: even with `internal=True` it runs the eval gate (`settings/save.py:444`) and `_apply_llm_tuple` creates a tuple of the still-active profile (`:299`), which would lie above the manifest's bound and block the profile's deletion.
- Produces:
  - `async def request_delete_everything(conn, *, now: datetime, idempotency_row_id: int, defer: Deferrer | None) -> OpenedPurge` (A0 with a `global` fence; the api route holds its idempotency row in the same transaction and passes that row's `id`, read inside `run_idempotent`'s handler, Task 18). The manifest's request is `{"idempotency_row_id": <id>}`, immutable. While one is `executing`, another is `Refusal(409, "conflict_state", detail="purge_in_progress")`.
  - `DELETE_EVERYTHING_KEEPS: Final[frozenset[str]]`, every table kept with its kept rows: the user account with its TOTP enrolment (M5-U3); the quota and spend ledgers, `llm_budget`, `quota_windows`; the policy and config tables of §20.1 without personal content (model policies, execution configs, evaluation policies, rubric and prompt-schema versions, sources and the default source instances); the singletons (`active_config`, `schedule_state`, `ranking_state`, `worker_heartbeat`, `deadman_state`, `schema_compat` and the transition matrix); `backup_sets`, `backup_runs`; manifests, shipments, shipment items and fences; the alerts open at A2 (step 11, frozen as ids); the new default settings and scope version; this purge's operation and this request's idempotency row; `alembic_version`.
  - `scoring/batch_results.py`: `PURGE_CANCEL_BACKOFF_S: Final = (1.0, 5.0)` (the waits after the first and the second failed try); `async def send_purge_cancel(client: BatchesClient, provider_batch_id: str, *, submission_id: int, tries: int = CANCEL_TRIES, sleep: Callable[[float], Awaitable[None]]) -> bool`, delete-everything's bounded sender. Each try retrieves the batch, then sends `messages.batches.cancel` only while it is `in_progress`. A `canceling` or ended batch is success with no send. A failed retrieval, a rejected cancel, or a client that is not configured (`LLMNotConfigured`, `llm/batches.py:347-350`) each count as one failed try. After `tries` failed tries it logs `batch_cancel_gave_up` with the submission and provider ids and returns `False`, and the caller deletes the row all the same (§20.4: "3 tries, then logged"). It returns `True` once a cancel is accepted or the batch no longer runs. Logs carry ids, statuses and error classes only.
  - `execute`: step 2's mapping of §20.4 exactly (runs `cancelled('delete_everything')`; cycles `failed('cancelled')`; other operations `cancelled` with `claim_id := NULL`, and the `query_proposals` of a cancelled `propose_queries` → `failed` in the same transaction (the M2 final review: such a proposal stayed `running`); submissions `preparing` → `abandon_preparing`, `submit_started`/`submit_ambiguous` → `released` with entries `dispatched → ambiguous`, `submitted`/`ended` → `record_cancel` with unsettled entries `dispatched → ambiguous`, and then, once that transaction has committed and before step 3 touches a submission, a live purge sends every recorded cancel itself through `batch_results.send_purge_cancel` (below), with `run.effects.batches_client` and no transaction open. It does not use `cancel_requested` (`scoring/batch_results.py:199-219`): that one returns at a failed retrieval before any try, which is right for the poller, whose next poll retries, but would lose the cancel here, where the row goes next. In replay (`run.effects is None`) no cancel is sent: each recorded one is dropped with `batch_cancel_skipped_replay` logged (submission and provider ids only), so replay needs no provider client (Deviation 56). Live purges leave this to the next poll (Task 11), because their submission rows stay; delete-everything deletes them, and a poll that finds no row sends nothing (`:140-143, 820-825`). Every submission still carrying `cancel_requested_at` when phase B (re)starts is sent again before step 3, so a crash between the recording and the send loses no cancel; a repeat finds the batch `canceling` and sends nothing; active bulk operations `cancelled`; every other unsettled entry through `lock_for_purge` and `settle_for_purge` (Task 11): `reserved` → `not_billed(never_dispatched)`, online `dispatched` → `ambiguous`, so no entry is left `reserved` or `dispatched` when step 3 deletes its call); then step 3's deletes 1–12 in order, each a `row_delete` with the `all` selector bounded by `max_ids` (the targets through `purge_target_set`, at most `TARGET_SET_CHUNK` a transaction, and any `delete_job_graph` call in a graph chunk holding the groups' co-members, as Task 13 runs them), with these explicit additions (P4, P19, D14, M5-U3): step 5 deletes `duplicate_groups` and every tombstone; step 7 is preceded, in a live purge, by one `run.effects.deadman.ping_pending()` (Task 5, when a pinger is configured; replay makes no ping), with no transaction open: a run that ended before the purge and is not pinged yet would otherwise lose its ping with its row, and healthchecks, seeing no ping for a whole interval, would raise a false dead-man alarm; step 8, in a `chunk(config="update")`, calls `settings.reset.reset_to_defaults` (below) and bumps `ranking_state` last; step 11 also deletes `login_attempts` (bounded by `id`), `login_lockouts` (bounded by its `generation`, Task 1, so a lockout taken or renewed after the manifest survives a re-execution or replay, and no IP address enters the manifest), and the `procrastinate_jobs` that are not in `doing` at A2, frozen there as an id list (`row_delete procrastinate_jobs ids=[…]`, their events cascading): a job `doing` at A2 is kept even after it finishes, so a finished job never falls into the deleted set later. It keeps this request's own `api_idempotency` row: `row_delete api_idempotency` is bounded by its new identity `id` (Task 1) with `keep=[request.idempotency_row_id]`. Step 11 deletes only the alerts resolved at A2, frozen there as an id list (`row_delete alerts ids=[…]`): an alert open at A2 is kept even when it is resolved before phase B or after completion. An open alert (`resolved_at IS NULL`) holds a code and ids, never content, and is the dispatcher's outbox, possibly not yet pushed. Two codes, `llm.reservation_bound_violated` and `llm.auth_failed`, also pause the score lane until acknowledged (`scoring/cycles.py:257-265`). Deleting an open alert would drop its push and lift that pause unacknowledged. An open alert that names a deleted row is resolved as its code's resolver says, or by hand. Step 12 deletes no session.
  - `settings/reset.py` *(new)*: `async def reset_to_defaults(conn, *, now: datetime) -> int` (the new settings version id), a dedicated path with no activation gate and no scoring work: the shipped default document (`seed.load_settings_v1()`, validated as `Settings`); `ensure_derived(conn, derived(settings, profile=None), now=now)` for its scope version, model policy, execution config and evaluation policy (reused by hash when they exist); one `settings_versions` row (the next `version_no`, `effect_classes = '{none}'`); one `UPDATE active_config` to those ids with `profile_version_id`, `llm_tuple_id` and `pending_scope_version_id` NULL (rubric and prompt-schema versions unchanged); `schedule_state` reset as `save_settings` resets it, pointing at the new version (P4). It creates no tuple, no operation and no bulk operation, and the caller holds `active_config FOR UPDATE` and bumps the ranking (Deviation 24). The kind's postcondition for step 8 uses the manifest's immutable sets, so later activity keeps it true. Every pointer of `active_config` must be NULL, above its table's bound, or in the manifest's `retained` set for that table, and so must `schedule_state.settings_version_id`. The pointers are `settings_version_id`, `scope_version_id`, `pending_scope_version_id`, `model_policy_id`, `execution_config_id`, `evaluation_policy_id`, `rubric_version_id`, `prompt_schema_version_id`, `profile_version_id` and `llm_tuple_id`. A2 freezes `retained`:
    - `scope_versions`: every scope version within the bound that step 10 does not list. That includes each row the reset may reuse by hash and each row a kept row references. A settings save that later hashes back to one of them (`ensure_hashed`, `db/seed.py:186-200`) may put it in `scope_version_id` or, while it is applied, in `pending_scope_version_id` (`settings/scope_apply.py:224-237`), and stays satisfied.
    - `model_policies`, `execution_configs`, `evaluation_policies`, `rubric_versions` and `prompt_schema_versions`: every row within the bound, these tables being kept whole and the reset reusing their rows by hash through `ensure_derived`.
    - `settings_versions`, `profile_versions` and `llm_tuples`: none. Settings versions are never reused, and the purge deletes every profile version and tuple within the bound, so a later confirm or tuple is a new row above it.
    A dump from before the purge fails this check (its pointers name deleted rows), which is what replay must fix.
  - Step 3's other memberships, frozen the same way (Task 9's rule):

    - step 10's "scope versions only they used" are listed by id at A2: the scope versions within the bound that no kept row references. That list leaves out the rows `reset_to_defaults` will reuse by hash, because `derived(seed.load_settings_v1(), profile=None)` is computed at A2 too, so step 8's reuse never meets a deleted row;

      - **`created_at ≤ fenced_at`:** `ad_keys` and `sessions`.
      - **`thread`:** the checkpoint tables (Task 9).
      - **Id lists frozen at A2:** `alerts`, `procrastinate_jobs` (a `bigserial`, which Task 23 now advances past every listed id; its events cascade, and procrastinate's own delete trigger unlinks a periodic defer, `procrastinate_unlink_periodic_defers_v1`) and `scope_versions`.
  - The final transaction deletes every session created at or before the manifest's `fenced_at` (the requester's too: the user is logged out), releases the global fence, checks the postconditions and marks the manifest `completed` (D14; Task 12's phase C). No rebuild.

### Task 17: Export

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/export.py`, `tests/unit/test_export_segments.py`, `tests/integration/test_export.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/retention.py` (rule `exports`), `worker/main.py` (`_REGISTERING_MODULES` gains `jobhunter.privacy.export`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `crypto.encrypt(key, plaintext, *, aad)`, `crypto.decrypt(keys, blob, *, aad)`, `data_keys(cfg)` (`crypto.py:61, 69, 93`); `CvStore.read` (`profile/storage.py:95`); `exports` (Task 1) and its `UNIQUE exports_one_running`; `create_operation`, `register_operation_handler(kind, on_failed=)` (`worker/operations.py:244`), whose hook runs in every transaction that ends the operation `failed`: a `failed` finish, the tenth requeue and an exhausted reclaim or takeover (`_release`, `:549-563`); `OperationContext.wait_in` (Task 8); `is_fenced`; `ids.uuid7`; `cfg.exports_dir`.
- Produces:
  - `EXPORT_TTL: Final = timedelta(days=7)`, `SEGMENT_BYTES: Final = 1 << 20`, `EXPORT_FILES: Final = ("profile_versions.json", "settings_versions.json", "jobs.json", "scores.json", "feedback.json", "golden_cases.json", "runs.json", "README.txt")` plus the `cv_files/` directory.
  - `async def request_export(conn, *, now: datetime, defer: Deferrer | None) -> int` (an `exports` row `running` with `expires_at = now + 7 d` and an `export` operation, payload `{"export_id": id}`, dedup key `export`; a second one while one runs → `Refusal(409, "conflict_state", detail="export_running")`, from the unique index).
  - Handler `run_export(ctx)`: reads in one `REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY` snapshot whose first statements read the purge generation `g = max(deletion_manifests.id)` (0 when none) and whether any manifest is `executing`; one is → no content is read and the operation waits (`ctx.wait_in(error_code="purge_in_progress", not_before=now + 1 min)`, no attempt spent, so a long purge never exhausts it; an export still waiting `EXPORT_WAIT_LIMIT: Final = timedelta(hours=24)` after its `created_at` ends `failed` instead, `ctx.finish_in(status="failed", error_code="purge_in_progress")`, because a failed purge keeps its manifest `executing` until the user retries it), so an export never snapshots data a purge is deleting (admission serialised with every purge, the fence-free golden-case delete included); leaves out every fenced job (L6560); writes the zip on the worker's `/tmp` tmpfs; encrypts it to `<exports_dir>/<id>-<uuid7>.jhx.tmp` in segments of 1 MiB, each `crypto.encrypt(data_key, chunk, aad=f"export:{id}:{n}:{int(last)}".encode())` framed as a 4-byte big-endian length and the blob; fsync, rename, fsync of the directory; removes the plaintext. Final transaction (§20.3 L6508): `active_config FOR SHARE` (every purge's A0 inserts its manifest under `active_config FOR UPDATE`, so this read is serialised with it); an active fence, or a manifest with an id above `g` (a purge opened after the snapshot, fenced or not) → the export `purged`, the file unlinked; else `running → ready` with `storage_path`, `sha256` (of the stored file), `size_bytes` (the zip's size) and `purge_generation = g`. It moves only from `running`: a purge that marked it first wins (D9, Deviation 19), and the handler then unlinks the file it wrote, which nothing names. A handler that finds its export no longer `running` when it starts (`purged`, or `failed` by a restore) ends `succeeded` at once, writing nothing. Each attempt first unlinks the `<id>-*` files of the export's earlier attempts, which a crash after the rename can leave and no row names.
  - **Export states**, the one table that live purges, replay and restore all follow (the edges are Task 1's, unchanged: `running → ready | failed | purged`, `ready → expired | purged`; nothing leaves `failed`, `expired` or `purged`). The three content-free states have `storage_path` NULL and no file:

    | State | Files on the exports volume | `storage_path` | `?download=1` | A purge's export op, live or replayed | `restore run`, after replay |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | `running` | at most its own `<id>-*.jhx.tmp` or unpublished `<id>-*.jhx` | NULL | 409 | → `purged`; `<id>-*` removed | → `failed`; the volume emptied |
    | `ready` | `<id>-<uuid7>.jhx` | set | 200 | → `purged`, `storage_path` NULL; the file removed | → `expired`, `storage_path` NULL; the volume emptied |
    | `failed` | none | NULL | 409 | left as it is | left as it is |
    | `expired` | none | NULL | 410 | left as it is | left as it is |
    | `purged` | none | NULL | 410 | left as it is | left as it is |

    A purge's export op names the `running` and `ready` exports at A2. When phase B (or a replay) reaches a named row, it may have become `failed` (the failure hook) or `expired` (the expiry) meanwhile, or it may be in any state in a restored dump; such a row is already content-free, so the op leaves it and only removes `<id>-*`. Restore applies its column after replay (Task 26), so replay never meets a row that restore moved. The expiry sets `storage_path` NULL with `expired`; the failure hook moves only `running` rows, which never have one.
  - The failure hook `export_failed(conn, operation_id, payload, error_code)` (`register_operation_handler("export", on_failed=export_failed)`): the payload's export `running → failed`, a row in any other state left as it is (idempotent: a purge or the publication moved it first), and its files unlinked (`<id>-*.jhx.tmp` and an unpublished `<id>-*.jhx`; nothing names them, so a rolled-back hook loses nothing). It covers every way the operation can end `failed`, so no `running` row outlives its operation and `exports_one_running` never blocks the next export.
  - `def iter_decrypted(source: BinaryIO, keys: Sequence[bytes], *, export_id: int) -> Iterator[bytes]` (an open file, so a reader that opened it keeps reading after an unlink) (checks the segment order and the final flag; a truncated, reordered or foreign file raises `ExportCorrupt`).
  - Zip content: `profile_versions.json` (the retained versions' contact-stripped profiles), `cv_files/<cv_file_id>.<ext>` (decrypted originals that still exist; a pasted CV as `.txt`), `settings_versions.json`, `jobs.json` (canonical fields, user actions, notes), `scores.json` (current and historical results with evidence, critic notes and versions), `feedback.json`, `golden_cases.json`, `runs.json` (reports), `README.txt` (what each file holds, the 7-day availability, a pointer to the privacy statement).
  - Retention rule `exports`: a `ready` export past `expires_at` → `expired` with `storage_path` NULL, its file unlinked after that commit; a `running` export whose operation is neither `pending` nor `running` (lost without its hook) → `failed`, its files unlinked; a file of the exports directory older than one day that no `ready` row names (`<id>-*.jhx` or `.jhx.tmp`) is unlinked, as `cv_store_orphans` does for CV files; `expired`, `failed` and `purged` rows deleted 30 days after `expires_at` (Deviation 48).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** `test_export_segments.py`: `test_segments_round_trip`; `test_a_truncated_file_raises`; `test_reordered_segments_raise`; `test_the_aad_binds_the_export_id`. `test_export.py`: `test_an_export_holds_every_file_and_decrypts`; `test_a_fenced_job_is_absent_from_the_export`; `test_an_export_meeting_a_fence_ends_purged`; `test_an_export_waits_while_a_purge_is_executing` (a golden-case delete opened and not completed: the export waits `purge_in_progress`, reads nothing and spends no attempt, however many minutes pass); `test_an_export_waiting_past_24_hours_fails`; `test_an_exhausted_export_fails_and_a_new_export_succeeds` (ten failing attempts: the hook moves it `failed` and removes its files; the next `request_export` is accepted and ends `ready`); `test_an_export_reclaimed_on_its_last_attempt_fails_through_the_hook`; `test_a_named_export_that_failed_or_expired_before_phase_b_satisfies_the_op` (named at A2, then `failed` by its hook or `expired` by retention before phase B: the purge completes, the row unchanged, no `<id>-*` file left); `test_retention_fails_a_running_export_without_a_live_operation`; `test_a_purge_that_marks_a_running_export_first_leaves_no_file` (the handler's final transaction finds it `purged` and unlinks its own file); `test_an_attempt_removes_an_earlier_attempts_archive` (a crash after the rename, then a successful retry: one file, the published one); `test_a_handler_whose_export_is_no_longer_running_writes_nothing`; `test_retention_removes_an_unnamed_archive`; `test_a_purge_opened_after_the_snapshot_purges_the_export_at_publication` (a fence-free golden-case delete opened and completed between the export's snapshot and its final transaction: the export ends `purged`, its file gone); `test_a_purge_after_ready_deletes_it`; `test_one_export_at_a_time`; `test_it_expires_after_7_days_and_the_row_goes_after_30`; `test_the_stored_file_is_never_plaintext` (no `PK\x03\x04` in it).

- Produces:
  - `api/reauth.py`: `async def verify_reauth(engine: AsyncEngine, cfg: AppConfig, gate: Argon2Gate, *, ip: str, password: str, totp_code: str | None, now: datetime) -> None`: a lockout in force → 429 `throttled`, as login answers it; the password verified through the gate; with TOTP enabled, the code must match an unused step (`claim_totp_step`); any failure records one failed attempt (`serialize`, then `record_attempt(success=False)`, which can lock the address out) and answers 403 `reauth_failed`; success records nothing (it is no login) (M5-U4, D13; Deviations 3, 21).
  - Bodies: `DeleteEverythingIn{confirm: str, password: str, totp: str | None}`, `PurgeSourceIn{source: Literal["adzuna"], confirm: str, password: str, totp: str | None}`. Order: validation (422 `validation_failed`); `confirm` not exactly `"DELETE EVERYTHING"` / `"PURGE ADZUNA"` → 422 `confirmation_mismatch`, nothing recorded; the action's fingerprint, `request_fingerprint` of the body **without** `password` and `totp` (a credential is no part of the action, so a retry with a new code is the same request); `api_idempotency.stored_response(conn, *, route, key, fingerprint, now) -> StoredResponse | None` (new: a completed row with that fingerprint → its stored response, returned at once without a second re-authentication — the first request was authenticated, and this one carries a session too; another fingerprint → 422 `idempotency_key_reused`; `in_progress` → 409 `idempotency_in_progress`; none or expired → None); only then re-auth, and A0 inside `run_idempotent` with the same fingerprint (for delete-everything, the handler reads its own claimed row's `id`, `SELECT id FROM api_idempotency WHERE route = :route AND idem_key = :key`, and passes it as `idempotency_row_id`, Task 16) (two concurrent first requests: the loser of `run_idempotent`'s claim gets 409 or the stored response). So a lost response retried with the same key and a TOTP step already used returns the stored 202 (Deviation 21).
  - Routes (`require_session`; the POSTs need CSRF; the three keyed ones need `Idempotency-Key`):
    - `POST /api/privacy/export` (keyed) → 202 `{export_id}`; 409 `conflict_state` (`export_running`);
    - `GET /api/privacy/exports/{export_id}` → 200 `ExportOut{id, status, created_at, expires_at, size_bytes}`; with `?download=1` on a `ready` export → 200 `application/zip` streamed from `iter_decrypted`; before the response starts the route opens the file and decrypts its first segment, and a file gone meanwhile (a purge's export op or the expiry between the status read and the open) re-reads the row: `purged` or `expired` → 410 `gone`, anything else 500; once the 200 has started, the stream reads from the open descriptor, so an unlink cannot truncate a download already under way; `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="jobhunter-export-<id>.zip"`, logged `export_downloaded` with the id; `?download=1` on `running` or `failed` → 409 `conflict_state`; `expired` or `purged` → 410 `gone`; unknown → 404;
    - `POST /api/privacy/delete-everything` (keyed) → 202 `{manifest_id}`; 422 `confirmation_mismatch`; 403 `reauth_failed`; 409 `conflict_state` (`purge_in_progress`);
    - `GET /api/privacy/manifests` → `{items: list[ManifestOut], next_cursor}`, newest first (sort `privacy.manifests.id_desc`) (D18);
    - `GET /api/privacy/manifests/{manifest_id}` → `ManifestOut{id, kind, status, created_at, completed_at, shipped_at, deleted_golden_case_ids: list[int]}` (the last read from a source purge's `scope`);
    - `POST /api/privacy/purge-source` (keyed) → 202 `{manifest_id}`, the same errors;
    - `POST /api/system/purges/{operation_id}/retry` (not keyed, as retry-apply) → 202 `{operation_id, manifest_id}`: a new purge operation with the same payload and dedup key; 404 when the id is no purge operation; 409 `conflict_state` when its manifest is not `executing` or one of its purge operations is `pending` or `running`; the fence stays active throughout (D6);
    - `DELETE /api/golden-cases/{case_id}` → 202 `{manifest_id}` (U8); 404; 409 `conflict_state` (`eval_run_running`).
  - `ErrorCode` gains `confirmation_mismatch`, `reauth_failed`, `gone` (EN/DE text).
  - `BannerCode` gains `deletion_unshipped` (warn): any manifest `completed` and not `shipped` (§15.1 L5171).
  - `AttentionOut.failed_purges: {count, items: list[{manifest_id, kind, created_at, operation_id: int | None, last_error_code: str | None, attempts: int}]}`: one item per `executing` manifest whose newest purge operation is `failed`, or which has none (D8, P24).
  - `M5_ROUTES` rows: `("post", "/api/privacy/export"): {"400", "401", "403", "409", "413", "422", "429"}`; `("get", "/api/privacy/exports/{export_id}"): {"401", "404", "409", "410", "422", "429"}`; `("post", "/api/privacy/delete-everything")` and `("post", "/api/privacy/purge-source")`: `{"400", "401", "403", "409", "413", "422", "429"}`; `("get", "/api/privacy/manifests"): {"400", "401", "422", "429"}`; `("get", "/api/privacy/manifests/{manifest_id}"): {"401", "404", "422", "429"}`; `("post", "/api/system/purges/{operation_id}/retry"): {"401", "403", "404", "409", "422", "429"}`; `("delete", "/api/golden-cases/{case_id}"): {"401", "403", "404", "409", "422", "429"}`; the keyed set is `{export, delete-everything, purge-source}`.

### Task 19: `docs/PRIVACY.md`

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: `docs/PRIVACY.md` with these headings, in this order (the SPA anchors them): `## 1. Purpose and controller`, `## 2. Data categories`, `## 3. Processing and minimisation`, `## 4. Third parties and transfers`, `## 5. Retention`, `## 6. Deletion and export`, `## 7. Adzuna`, `## 8. GeoNames`, `## 9. Security measures`, `## 10. How to report a concern`, `## Operator`. It states:
  - §3: contact stripping and the user's review of the text before any LLM or embedding call; the stripper misses names on mixed header lines and glued URLs, so the user's review is the guarantee; the native-PDF opt-in and its disclosure; no inference of protected attributes; embeddings computed locally;
  - §4: §20.6's third-party table, word for word;
  - §5: §20.1's table, with exports "7 days; deleted by every user-requested purge and by delete-everything, not by the nightly job tombstoning, so an export can hold content tombstoned after it was made until it expires" (Deviation 18);
  - §6: export (a zip encrypted at rest with the data key, downloadable for 7 days over TLS, each download logged by id, never copied into a backup, so a restore does not bring it back); delete everything (the typed phrase, the password and, when TOTP is on, its code; it keeps the account and its TOTP enrolment, the ledgers, the configuration, the backup records and the manifests, and deletes login attempts and lockouts); the Adzuna purge; a deletion counts as done only when shown "confirmed offsite"; backup sets age out within about 3 months; the manifest replay keeps deleted data from returning on a restore;
  - §7: Adzuna's personal-use terms, its attribution and the purge; §8: the GeoNames CC BY 4.0 attribution; §9: §16 in summary (TLS, CSP, Argon2id, sessions, CSRF, throttling, the api without egress, LUKS where offered, the app's AES-256-GCM encryption, restic encryption, the offline recovery kit); §10: concerns go to the operator contact;
  - `## Operator`: five fields, each `[set at deployment: operator name]`, `[set at deployment: contact address]`, `[set at deployment: controller name]`, `[set at deployment: VPS provider]`, `[set at deployment: S3 backup provider]`, which the RUNBOOK's deploy checklist fills (Task 31, M5-R3).
  - Only headings, paragraphs, bullet lists, tables, `**bold**` and `` `code` `` (Task 20's renderer); no HTML, images or link syntax.

backup (`command: ["jobhunter", "backup", "scheduler"]`) and restore (`profiles: [restore]`, `command: ["jobhunter", "restore", "run"]`, `depends_on` postgres healthy) with the x-app hardening as `10001:10001` + group 10010 (not root; Deviation 29), `hostname: jobhunter-backup` on both (R13); `mem_limit` backup 1g, migrate 2g, restore 2g (R28); pinned networks (R10): `proxy` 172.30.0.0/24 (`ip_range` 172.30.0.128/25, gateway .1, Caddy .2), `edge` 172.30.1.0/24 (`ip_range` .128/25, Caddy .2), `internal` 172.30.2.0/24, `egress` 172.30.3.0/24; volumes as §19.1, except that `migrate` and `restore` also mount `exports` read-write, which §19.1 gives neither: migrate's replay and pending retries remove export archives (Task 25), and `restore run` empties the volume (Task 26; Deviation 55); secrets `file: ${JOBHUNTER_SECRETS_DIR:-/etc/jobhunter/secrets}/<name>` for all 21 names.

  - **Named volumes, their owners and modes** (one table for `compose.yaml`, `compose.test.yaml`, `compose.s3stub.yaml` and the dev compose's M5 additions). A fresh named volume takes the owner and mode of the mount point in the image of the container that first mounts it. So every image that mounts a volume carries that mount point with the same owner and mode, and the order in which services start never matters:

    | Volume | Mount point | Owner and mode, from | Written by (uid) | Read-only by |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | `pgdata` | `/var/lib/postgresql/data` | `999:999`, the pgvector image; its entrypoint makes it 0700 at the first start (as the dev stack runs it, `user: "999:999"`) | postgres (999) | — |
    | `cv_store` | `/data/cv` | `10001:10001` 0700, the app `runtime` stage (`Dockerfile:47-49`), inherited by `backup` | api, worker, migrate, restore (10001) | backup |
    | `manifests` | `/data/manifests` | `10001:10001` 0700, `runtime` (Task 9), inherited by `backup` | worker, migrate, restore (10001) | backup, the dev shipper |
    | `exports` | `/data/exports` | `10001:10001` 0700, `runtime` (Task 9) | worker, migrate, restore (10001) | api |
    | `backup_staging` | `/data/staging` | `10001:10001` 0700, the `backup` stage (Task 10) | backup, restore, the dev shipper (10001) | — |
    | `restic_repo` (dev and E2E only) | `/data/restic-repo` | `10001:10001` 0700, the `backup` stage (Task 10) | the dev shipper (10001) | — |
    | `parse_jobs` | `/data/parse` | the tmpfs options `uid=0,gid=10011,mode=2770`, as the dev compose (`deploy/compose.dev.yaml:233-235`) | worker, docparse (group 10011) | — |
    | `caddy_data`, `caddy_config` | `/data`, `/config` | `root` 0755, the caddy image, which sets no `USER` | caddy (root, every capability dropped but `NET_BIND_SERVICE`: the owner writes without `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`) | — |
    | `s3data` (`jobhunter-s3stub`) | `/data` | `root`, with `user: "0:0"` set on `s3stub`, so the owner does not depend on the versitygw image's default user (to read at execution) | s3stub (0) | — |

  - `deploy/compose.s3stub.yaml` (project `jobhunter-s3stub`): `s3stub` from `${versitygw:?}` with `user: "0:0"` (the table above), `posix /data` on the volume `s3data`, port 7070, the root credentials `jobhunter-test` / `jobhunter-test-secret-0000000000` (their flag or variable names read from `versitygw --help` of v1.9.0 at execution), on the network `jobhunter-s3net` (`name: jobhunter-s3net`). It outlives the test project's `down -v`, as offsite storage outlives a lost VPS.
  - `tests/stack/stubs/http_recorder.py` (stdlib only): `POST /ntfy/<topic>` → 200; `GET /hc/ping/<uuid>` and `/hc/ping/<uuid>/fail` → 200 `OK`; `POST /hc/api/v3/checks/<uuid>` (stores `timeout`, `grace`) and `GET` of it → JSON with `status` `up` or `paused`; `POST …/pause` → 200; `GET /_log` → the recorded requests (method, path, `Title`, `Priority`, `Tags`, `Click`, whether `Authorization` or `X-Api-Key` was present, body length; never a header's value); `POST /_reset`.
  - `tests/support/prodstack.py`: `PT: list[str]`, `PB: list[str]`, `S3: list[str]`; `def other_stack_up() -> str | None` (the dev, M4 stacktest or another prod-test project holds 172.30.0.0/24); `def ensure_test_secrets() -> None` (`sudo scripts/test-secrets.sh`, `groupadd --gid 10010` when absent); `STACK_SERVICES: tuple[str, ...] = ("caddy", "api", "worker", "docparse", "stubsvc")` (their dependencies `migrate`, `postgres` and `embedder` start with them); `def up(argv, *services: str, build: bool = True) -> None` (`up -d --build --wait`, or `--no-build` with `build=False` for Task 27's `prev` image; `STACK_SERVICES` when none is named). `backup` is not among them before Task 24, whose scheduler its command runs; Task 24 adds it; `def down_v(argv) -> None`; `def s3_up() -> None` (`$S3 up -d --wait`: it creates the external network `jobhunter-s3net` that `backup` and `restore` join, so no `PT` or `PB` command naming either service, `run --no-deps` included, can start before it) and `def s3_down() -> None` (`$S3 down -v`); `@contextmanager def prod_session(*services: str) -> Iterator[None]`, which every stack module's fixture wraps: it skips when `other_stack_up()` names a holder, then `ensure_test_secrets()`, `s3_up()` before any `PT` command, `up(PT, *services)`; at the end, after a failure too, `down_v(PT)`, `down_v(PB)` and `s3_down()`; `def caddy_root_ca(argv) -> Path` (Caddy's internal root, copied out for TLS verification); `def stub_log(argv) -> list[dict[str, object]]`; `def psql(argv, sql: str) -> str` (the bootstrap role in the `postgres` service).
  - `api/edge.py`: `LOGIN_LIMIT_PER_IP: Final = (10, 60.0)`, `LOGIN_LIMIT_GLOBAL: Final = (100, 60.0)` (requests, seconds): a `POST /api/auth/login` over either window → 429 `throttled` with `Retry-After`, before the body is read and before any database work; in memory (one api replica). §16.3's database throttle is unchanged. ### Task 22: The egress rule, the scripted host setup, N1, N2 and V20

**Files:**
- Create: `deploy/firewall/docker-user.nft`, `deploy/firewall/jobhunter-egress.service`, `scripts/host-setup.sh`, `tests/unit/test_firewall_files.py`, `tests/unit/test_host_setup_script.py`, `tests/stack/test_prod_network.py`
- Modify: `docs/spikes/V20.md` (the compose test stack's part), `docs/spikes/V21.md` (the local N1 part; the VPS part stays the deployment step's)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the pinned subnets and addresses of `deploy/compose.yaml` (Task 21); `deploy/secrets.example`; `tests/support/prodstack.py`; the M0 V20/V21 dev records.
- Produces:
  - `deploy/firewall/docker-user.nft` (Deviation 35, R9): its own table `inet jobhunter_egress` (declared, deleted and redefined, so a reload is idempotent) with a `forward`-hook filter chain at priority `-10`, policy accept: `ct state established,related accept`; `ip saddr { 172.30.3.0/24, 172.30.1.2 } accept` (`egress` and Caddy's `edge` address); `ip saddr { 172.30.0.0/24, 172.30.2.0/24, 172.30.1.0/24 } ip daddr != 172.30.0.0/16 ct state new counter drop`. The networks run without IPv6 (A record only, Deviation 36). The file keeps §16.9's name though it no longer uses Docker's `DOCKER-USER` chain, so it works with Docker's iptables-nft and native nftables backends alike and needs no Docker chain at load time.
  - `deploy/firewall/jobhunter-egress.service`: `Before=docker.service`, `Type=oneshot`, `RemainAfterExit=yes`, `ExecStart=/usr/sbin/nft -f /opt/jobhunter/deploy/firewall/docker-user.nft`, `ExecStop=/usr/sbin/nft delete table inet jobhunter_egress`, `WantedBy=multi-user.target`.

Its synchronous callers stay as they are: the CLI (`cli.py:206-208`), the `migrated` fixture (`tests/support/fixtures.py:149`), `tests/crash/test_singleton.py:58`, `tests/integration/test_api_labelling.py:440`, `tests/integration/test_jobs_list_plans.py:49` and `tests/integration/test_migrate.py:60`; the asynchronous restore code awaits `run_migrate_async` (Tasks 25, 26) (R12, Deviation 38).
  - `privacy/identities.py`: `async def rewrite_missing_files(conn, manifest_dir: Path) -> list[UUID]` (step 1's first half: an `executing` row with `file_sha256`, or a `completed` or `shipped` row, whose file is missing gets it written from `scope` through `write_manifest_file`; `sha256(canonical_bytes(scope)) ≠ file_sha256` stops `migrate` with `ManifestConflict`); `async def manifest_bounds(conn, manifest_dir: Path) -> dict[str, int]` (per table, the largest `max_ids` and watermark value and the largest id of any op's `ids` or `keep` list, over every file of the directory and every `deletion_manifests.scope` with content; an id a manifest names by list must never be allocated again, or a replay would delete the new row); `async def advance_identities(conn, bounds: Mapping[str, int]) -> dict[str, int]` (for each identity column of `public`, and each `serial` or `bigserial` column too (`procrastinate_jobs.id`, which delete-everything names by list), its sequence's next allocatable value is read from the sequence itself: `last_value + 1` when `is_called`, else `last_value` (a fresh sequence has `last_value = 1` and `is_called = false`, so its next value is 1); the target is the largest of the column's current maximum and the bound; when the next allocatable value is at or below the target, `setval(pg_get_serial_sequence(table, column), target, true)`, so the next value is `target + 1`; otherwise nothing: never lowered, no `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock, tables already above every bound untouched; R25). Step 1 = rewrite, then advance the tables that exist (none on a first installation); step 3 = advance every table again. Step 5, `queue_and_checkpointer`, keeps its name and its place, and ends with the same idempotent `advance_identities(conn, await manifest_bounds(conn, manifest_dir))` over every table, once `_setup_queue` and `setup_checkpointer` (`db/migrate.py:102-104`) have created their schemas. On a first installation those schemas, and `procrastinate_jobs`' `bigserial` sequence, do not exist before step 5 (`procrastinate/sql/schema.sql:65-66`), so steps 1 and 3 cannot move it. A queue job enqueued after migrate could then take an id that a delete-everything manifest of the volume lists, break that manifest's postcondition, and make the next `migrate` replay the purge. The pass runs before `seed` and `replay`, and nothing allocates an id between the schemas' creation and it: `_setup_queue` and `setup_checkpointer` create schema only, and the checkpointer's `checkpoint_migrations` rows have no sequence.
  - Procrastinate stays at its pinned 3.10.0 schema, and its setup in step 5 is unchanged (a future version bump adds its migration scripts there; Carry-forward 3); step 5 additionally performs the advancement above.

**Files:**

- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/cli_backup.py`, `config.py` (the cron and retention settings), `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` (`croniter==6.2.4` direct), `privacy/retention.py` (rule `backup_records`), `tests/support/prodstack.py` (`STACK_SERVICES` gains `backup`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Restic`, `CommandRunner` (Task 10); `cfg.dsn(..., db_role="backup")`; the backup tables and their machine (Task 1); `/opt/pgclient/bin/{pg_dump,pg_restore}`; `croniter` 6.2.4 (in `uv.lock` already); `tests/support/prodstack.py`.
- Produces:
  - `async def run_backup_set(cfg: AppConfig, restic: Restic, runner: CommandRunner, *, clock) -> int` (§19.5 steps 1–7; the set id): (1) `backup_sets` `running` as `jobhunter_backup`; (2) a psycopg connection as `jobhunter_backup` opens `REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY`, `SET LOCAL idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 0`, `SELECT pg_export_snapshot()`, and reads `now()` (`db_snapshot_at`), `count(*)` of `deletion_manifests` and of every application table, keeping the transaction open; (3) `pg_dump -Fc --snapshot=<id> -f /data/staging/set/jobhunter.dump` as `jobhunter_backup` (the password in the child's env only), then the snapshot transaction commits; (4) `pg_restore --list` must succeed, sha256 and size recorded; (5) `/data/staging/set/backup_set.json` (set id, dump sha256, Alembic revision, `db_snapshot_at`, `manifest_count`, `row_counts`, CV file count, app version, and `data_key_ids`: the hex key ids (`crypto.key_id`'s 8 bytes, the header of every ciphertext) found in the snapshot's `cv_files.raw_text_enc` and sealed TOTP secret and in the first bytes of every CV file backed up — the keys a restore of this set needs; Deviation 31); (6) one `restic backup /data/staging/set /data/cv /data/manifests --tag set --tag set:<id>` (the subdirectory `set/`, so the restic cache at `/data/staging/.restic-cache` stays out of the snapshot); (7) `succeeded` with the snapshot id and the inventory, the staging files removed (the restic cache kept). Any failing step → `failed` with `error` = `<step>:<error class>` (no content), staging cleared; no alert (Task 7's reconciler raises it; O1). Every path named here is `cfg.staging_dir`, `cfg.cv_store_dir` or `cfg.manifests_dir`, whose container defaults these are; `test_backup_set.py` points them at temporary directories. `/opt/pgclient/bin` is the backup image's, so the tests' fake runner never executes it.
  - `async def run_prune(cfg, restic: Restic, engine, *, clock) -> int` (a `backup_runs` row of kind `prune`; `restic forget --tag set --keep-daily 7 --keep-weekly 4 --keep-monthly 3 --prune`, the numbers from `JOBHUNTER_BACKUP_KEEP_DAILY/_WEEKLY/_MONTHLY`; the `manifests` snapshots are never forgotten, because the filter is `--tag set`; R13).
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class BackupJob: name: Literal["set", "ship", "prune", "restore_test"]; cron: str`; the defaults `JOBHUNTER_BACKUP_CRON="15 1 * * *"`, `JOBHUNTER_MANIFEST_SHIP_CRON="*/5 * * * *"`, `JOBHUNTER_BACKUP_PRUNE_CRON="30 2 * * 0"` (new) and `JOBHUNTER_RESTORE_TEST_CRON="30 3 * * 0"` with the job running only when the day of the month is at most 7, the first Sunday (R15); `def next_due(jobs: Sequence[BackupJob], *, now: datetime) -> tuple[BackupJob, datetime]` (croniter, UTC); `async def run_scheduler(cfg, *, clock, sleep, run_job: Callable[[BackupJob], Awaitable[None]], stop: asyncio.Event) -> None`: one job at a time; a job that comes due while another runs waits, except `ship`, which is skipped when its next tick comes first; each run logged `backup_job` with the job, the outcome and the duration; a stop request ends it between jobs. The backup container's command is `jobhunter backup scheduler` (Deviation 29).
  - CLI: `jobhunter backup set`, `jobhunter backup prune`, `jobhunter backup scheduler` (each after `startup_or_exit("backup")`). The scheduler first ends every `backup_sets` and `backup_runs` row still `running` as `failed` (`error` = `interrupted`), its staging cleared: one backup container runs one job at a time, so a row running at its start was left by a container that died, and nothing else would ever end it (the health page would show it running for good).
  - Retention rule `backup_records` (`THIRTEEN_MONTHS`): finished `backup_runs`, then `backup_sets`, older than 13 months, never the newest succeeded set.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `parse_manifest`, `ManifestRow`, `check_postconditions`, `insert_fence`, `release_fences`, `complete_manifest` (Task 9); `KINDS[kind].execute` with `PurgeRun(mode="replay")` (Task 12); `KINDS[kind].reapply_admission` (Task 15); `rewrite_missing_files`, `advance_identities`, `run_migrate_async` (Task 23); `seed_two_year_old_graph` (Task 13); `tests/support/pg.py`'s server (its container runs `pg_dump` and `pg_restore`).
- Produces:
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Classified: manifest_uuid: UUID; outcome: Literal["replayed", "pending_retry", "checked", "satisfied"]`; `async def classify_stream(conn, manifest_dir: Path) -> list[Classified]` per §19.5's table, in `manifest_uuid` order (a file that does not parse or whose name differs from its uuid raises `ManifestInvalid`, which stops `migrate`; `checked` manifests whose postconditions hold are `satisfied`).
  - `async def replay_stream(engine, cfg: AppConfig, *, manifest_dir: Path, now: datetime) -> ReplayReport` (`ReplayReport(replayed: int, pending_completed: int, rebuilt_jobs: int)`): nothing to replay → returns; phase A: every replayed manifest's fences made active (reconstructed where absent) and, in the same transaction under `active_config FOR UPDATE`, its kind's `reapply_admission` (the source purge's disabling settings version, `purge_fence_at` and run cancels, Task 15; `execute` is steps 3–5 only); each pending retry completed from its row (content computed under the restored fence, file written: only rows without `file_sha256`); then each replayed manifest's kind `execute` in stream order with `mode="replay"` and `effects=None` (no Batches client, no pinger, no provider or healthchecks secret: replay changes the database and local files only, Deviation 56), recreating tombstones by re-running the tombstone kind on its stored ids (D4), and, for a kind with `deletes_exports`, `purge_exports(run, <its export ops>)` right after it, as `run_purge`'s step 3 runs them live (`execute` alone never removes an archive); the file ops and their postconditions use the export root migrate was given (`--exports-dir`, default the mounted `exports` volume), never an unmounted image directory, which would report a file absent while the archive remains; no rebuild between manifests; phase B: `source_purge.rebuild_jobs(run, job_ids)` once over the union of the replayed source purges' stored affected jobs, minus deleted jobs, following `merged_into_job_id` to the root, with each job's current det instances recomputed for the active and pending scopes; phase C: one transaction releases the replayed manifests' fences, then checks every postcondition of every replayed manifest (the `fence` ops' "no active fence" now holds; Task 12's order), marks them `completed` where needed and appends `replay_log` (`{restore_id, at, counts}`); a violation rolls the transaction back, so every fence stays active, and raises `ReplayFailed` (migrate stops). A crash anywhere restarts step 7 from the stored sets. `migrate` writes files (step 1, pending retries) but never deletes or overwrites a valid one. Fault points: `privacy.replay.after_a`, `privacy.replay.after_b`.
  - `jobhunter manifests replay [--manifest-dir DIR]`: steps 1, 3 and 7 alone (§19.4).
  - `tests/support/restore.py`: `async def dump_and_restore(server: PgServer, *, source_db: str, target_db: str) -> None` (`pg_dump -Fc` and `pg_restore --no-owner --no-acl --exit-on-error` inside the server's container, as the migrator).

### Task 27: Rollback by compatibility floor and the upgrade-then-rollback drill

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/db/migrations/versions/0011_m5_purge_operation_index.py`, `tests/integration/test_migration_0011.py`, `tests/integration/test_schema_compat.py`, `tests/support/fake_revision/` (a test-only revision directory), `scripts/build-prev-image.sh`, `tests/stack/test_prod_drills.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/db/compat.py` (`schema_state`), `db/migrate.py`, `observability/health.py` (readiness), `tests/unit/test_compat_floors.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `schema_compat` and `chain_compat` (Task 1); `check_readiness` (`observability/health.py:216`); `code_head()` (`db/alembic_runner.py`); `tests/support/prodstack.py`.
- Produces:
  - `SchemaState = Literal["current", "behind", "ahead_compatible", "ahead_incompatible"]`; `async def schema_state(conn, *, code_head: str) -> SchemaState`: the database's head equals the code's → `current`; the head is in the code's chain → `behind`; otherwise the database's `schema_compat` row for its head: a floor in the code's chain at or before the code's head, and not `destructive` → `ahead_compatible`; a destructive head, a floor the code does not know, or no row → `ahead_incompatible` (M5-R4 R2, Deviation 32).
  - `migrate`: `behind` → `alembic upgrade head`; `ahead_compatible` → the alembic step is skipped (`migrate_schema_ahead_compatible` logged with both heads) and the other steps run; `ahead_incompatible` → exit 1 with `migrate_schema_ahead_incompatible` before any step (the RUNBOOK then rolls back by restore). `/readyz`'s `migrations` passes for `current` and `ahead_compatible`. The idempotent seed of an older release writes nothing over a newer schema.
  - `0011_m5_purge_operation_index.py` (a real expand migration, the drill's subject): `CREATE INDEX operations_purge_manifest ON operations ((payload ->> 'manifest_id')) WHERE kind = 'purge'` (the purge-fences reconciler, the retry and the failed-purges section read by it; the table is small, so no `CONCURRENTLY`); `compat_floor = "0010_m5_operations"`, `destructive = False`. A destructive migration declares `destructive = True` and itself as floor.
  - `scripts/build-prev-image.sh`: builds the app image of the parent of the commit that added 0011 (found with `git log --diff-filter=A --format=%H -- <0011's path>`, so it needs the full history: `prodstack.yml` checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, Task 29) in a `git worktree` under `$(mktemp -d)`, removed afterwards, and tags it `jobhunter-app:prev`.
  - The drill (`test_prod_drills.py::test_upgrade_then_rollback`): PT on `app_image=jobhunter-app:prev` with data (fixtures collection, a profile). Every `prev` step sets `app_image=jobhunter-app:prev` in the environment, which takes precedence over `compose.test.env`, and starts without building (`up(PT, build=False)`, `--no-build`): the test overlay's `build:` would otherwise rebuild the current code under that tag. Then §19.6's deploy of the 0011 image (`$PT run --rm migrate`, `$PT up -d --wait`, on `jobhunter-app:dev` built from the current code); then the rollback (`app_image=jobhunter-app:prev`: `migrate` succeeds as `ahead_compatible`, `up -d --wait` healthy, `/readyz` 200, login and the Jobs list work, the data intact); then `schema_compat.destructive` set for 0011 in the database: `prev`'s `migrate` exits `migrate_schema_ahead_incompatible` and `/readyz` answers 503 `migrations`; finally `down -v` and `docker image rm jobhunter-app:prev`.

### Task 28: Key rotation: `crypto rotate`, the DB role rotation and the rotation drill

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/crypto_rotate.py`, `scripts/rotate-db-role.sh`, `tests/integration/test_crypto_rotate.py`, `tests/unit/test_rotate_db_role_script.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/cli.py` (`crypto rotate`), `tests/stack/test_prod_drills.py` (`test_rotation_drill`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `crypto.key_id`, `encrypt`, `decrypt`, `data_keys(cfg)` (`crypto.py:50-93`); the CV store's files and `cv_files.raw_text_enc` (`profile/storage.py`); the user's sealed TOTP secret (`auth/users.py:219`); the export file format and `iter_decrypted` (Task 17); the `data_key_previous` secret (declared for api and worker, Task 21); `deploy/postgres/init/10-init.sh:13-22` (passwords read into psql variables, never on a command line).
- Produces:
  - `ROTATION_BATCH: Final = 100`; `@dataclass(frozen=True) class RotationReport: rewritten: dict[str, int]; remaining_by_key_id: dict[str, int]` (hex key id → ciphertexts still under it, the current key's left out); `async def rotate(cfg: AppConfig, engine: AsyncEngine, *, batch: int = ROTATION_BATCH) -> RotationReport`: every ciphertext under the mounted `data_key_previous`'s key id (CV files on disk, `cv_files.raw_text_enc`, the TOTP secret, export files) re-encrypted with `data_key` in batches, each row or file once; a ciphertext under any other old key is left as it is and counted, since `crypto.decrypt` has no key for it (`crypto.py:83`) — a set taken during one rotation and restored after the next holds two keys, neither current; resumable and idempotent by key id; then a verification pass counts every ciphertext by key id (§16.8 L5490). `jobhunter crypto rotate` (the worker's role: `docker compose run --rm --no-deps worker jobhunter crypto rotate`) prints the report (key ids and counts, nothing else) and exits 0 when only the current key remains; 3 with `other_keys_remain` when ciphertexts under keys it was not given remain (the next pass mounts one of them as `data_key_previous`); 2 with `no_previous_key` when `data_key_previous` is empty and a non-current ciphertext exists. The two key slots thus recover a set under any number of old keys, one pass per key (Deviation 31).
  - `scripts/rotate-db-role.sh [--compose '<command line>'] [--secrets-dir DIR] [--dry-run] <secret-name> <new-file>` (run as root; `<secret-name>` one of `db_app`, `db_migrator`, `db_backup`, `db_restore`). `--compose` defaults to Task 21's prod command line (`docker compose -p jobhunter --env-file deploy/images.lock --env-file deploy/release.lock --env-file /etc/jobhunter/jobhunter.env -f deploy/compose.yaml`) and `--secrets-dir` to `/etc/jobhunter/secrets`, so the RUNBOOK calls it without either; the drill passes PT's command line and `.secrets/test`, the directory `compose.test.env` mounts, since none of the prod defaults exists on the test host. `--dry-run` prints each command with names, never a value. Steps: `install -o root -g 10010 -m 0440 <new-file> <secrets-dir>/<secret-name>`; then a one-off `<compose> run --rm --no-deps postgres sh -c '…'` (inside the `sh -c`, `psql -h postgres -U "$POSTGRES_USER" -d postgres`, so the variable and the password file expand in the container, never on the host) that reads the new password into a psql variable from the freshly mounted `/run/secrets/<secret-name>` and runs `ALTER ROLE <role> PASSWORD :'pw'` (the bootstrap password from `pg_bootstrap` in the one-off's env only); then `<compose> restart` of the services holding it (`db_app`: api, worker; `db_backup`, `db_restore`: backup; `db_migrator`: none, migrate and restore are one-off). It prints role and service names only (R7, Deviation 39).
  - The rotation drill (`test_prod_drills.py::test_rotation_drill`, on PT, every secret file written into `.secrets/test`, PT's secrets directory): R6 — after `install` of a new `session_secret` the running api still sees the old inode (`stat -c %i /run/secrets/session_secret`) and after `$PT restart api` the new one, and the old session cookie answers 401; the data key — a backup set taken first (`$PT exec backup jobhunter backup set`, its `data_key_ids` naming the old key), then the old key mounted as `data_key_previous`, the new as `data_key`, `restart api worker`, `crypto rotate` exits 0 (only the new key remains), a CV still decrypts, `data_key_previous` emptied, restart, the CV still decrypts; `db_app` rotated with `sudo scripts/rotate-db-role.sh --compose '<PT command line>' --secrets-dir .secrets/test db_app <file>`: PT's `api` and `worker` restarted (their containers' `StartedAt` moved), no container of another project restarted (the `S3` project's `StartedAt` unchanged), the role's old password refused and the new one accepted by PT's `postgres`, `/readyz` 200 and login works; then, after `$PT down -v` (PB pins the same subnets), the old backup set restored into PB with the new key as `data_key` and the old key from the recovery kit as `data_key_previous` (the set's `data_key_ids` name it): `crypto rotate` on B exits 0 and the restored CV decrypts — a set from before a rotation stays recoverable only with the kit's historical key (Deviation 31).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** `test_crypto_rotate.py`: `test_every_ciphertext_kind_is_reencrypted` (a CV file, a raw text, the TOTP secret, an export); `test_it_is_resumable_and_idempotent` (interrupted after one batch, run again); `test_the_verification_counts_remaining_ciphertexts_by_key_id`; `test_without_a_previous_key_it_refuses`; `test_a_set_under_keys_a_and_b_is_recovered_under_c_in_two_passes` (current key C; A mounted as previous → exit 3, `remaining_by_key_id` names B only; B mounted → exit 0, every ciphertext under C, a CV and the TOTP secret of each original key decrypt). `test_rotate_db_role_script.py`: `test_the_password_never_reaches_a_command_line`; `test_it_restarts_the_holders_of_each_secret` (its dry-run output); `test_the_defaults_are_the_prod_command_line_and_secrets_directory`; `test_compose_and_secrets_dir_replace_both` (the dry-run with PT's command line and `.secrets/test` names neither `/etc/jobhunter` nor the project `jobhunter`).

### Task 31: `docs/RUNBOOK.md`

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
