- Produces: `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, its new sections in this order before the existing ones (§19.8; R30, M3 Ruling T27-a). Their compose commands, written `docker compose …` below, are `$JH …` in the file: `JH` is set once at the top of the new sections to Task 21's prod command line (`docker compose -p jobhunter --env-file deploy/images.lock --env-file deploy/release.lock --env-file /etc/jobhunter/jobhunter.env -f deploy/compose.yaml`, from `/opt/jobhunter`), since a bare `docker compose` reads neither lock file and `${app_image:?}` stops it:
  1. **Deploy checklist**, each input with where it goes: ntfy server, topic and token (`NTFY_TOPIC_URL`, `ntfy_token`); healthchecks (one check for the collection schedule, its UUID ping URL, a read-write API key, `HEALTHCHECKS_API_URL` for a self-hosted server, and healthchecks' own alert integration: the only path that works when the VPS is dead); LangSmith (an EU workspace, a service key in `langsmith_key`, the project `jobhunter-prod`, its trace retention and quota); the VPS (provider, EU region, 4 vCPU, 16 GB, ≥ 100 GB NVMe, OS image, LUKS where offered, a pinned Docker Engine version no older than the one N1's DNS assertion passed on); domain and DNS (an A record only, Deviation 36); the ACME e-mail; the offsite S3 provider, bucket and keys (bucket versioning with a noncurrent-version expiry longer than the retention window, no compliance-mode Object Lock, R33); the SSH model (key-only; no Claude session gets access); GHCR (run `release.yml`, merge its digest PR, check out the release tag); where the offline recovery kit is kept; the embedder's fp32 or int8 after V7; PRIVACY.md's five operator fields; the exclusivity confirmations (the SerpApi account used by this app only, C2; the Anthropic workspace by this installation only, C3).
  2. **Host setup**: `sudo scripts/host-setup.sh --secrets-from <dir> --delete-sources`, and its manual equivalent (§19.8 steps 1–6, `startup-check --role <service>` for api, worker, migrate, backup and restore; R22).
  3. **First start**: `docker compose run --rm backup jobhunter backup init` (restic init, explicit, once); `up -d`; `docker compose run --rm api jobhunter user create --username <name>` (it asks for the password twice; `--password-stdin` is refused in prod, `cli_user.py:115`); `/readyz` from outside; V15 (`docker compose run --rm --no-deps worker jobhunter ops notify-test`, `… ops deadman-sync --show`); V20, V21 and V7 as the deployment step's procedure lists them.
  4. **Start and stop** (never `--scale worker`), and **a frozen worker**: with one replica a paused or swap-storming worker is never replaced; `docker compose restart worker`, the takeover does the rest; the dead-man switch is what pages (M0 Carry-forward 3).
  5. **Health check** (§19.8).
  6. **Alerts**: one row per catalogue code, its action (the ten rows of §19.8, `source.auth_failed` with `docker compose restart worker` (R6); `docparse.leftover`: "recreate the worker and docparse containers"; `profile.extract_generation_mismatch`: how to fail the draft by hand; `deadman.update_failed`, `notify.delivery_failed`, `backup.*`, `system.*`, `worker.heartbeat_stale`, `embedder.checksum_mismatch`, `privacy.purge_failed` (System → Needs attention → Retry), `privacy.manifest_unshipped` (`docker compose exec backup jobhunter backup ship`); manual codes resolved in System → Alerts).
  7. **Secret rotation**: `install -o root -g 10010 -m 0440`, then `docker compose restart <services>` (never `up -d` alone: Compose does not see a file's content, R6); the session secret logs the user out; the data key with `data_key_previous` and `jobhunter crypto rotate`; a DB role with `scripts/rotate-db-role.sh` (R7).
  8. **Backups and the offline recovery kit** (R1): what the kit holds (the current `data_key` and every earlier data key a retained backup set may still need, each labelled with its key id; `restic_password`, `s3_access_key_id`, `s3_secret_access_key`, `RESTIC_REPOSITORY`, `/etc/jobhunter/jobhunter.env`), that it is kept offline at the place the checklist names, and that only the kit restores a lost VPS. A key retired by a rotation stays in the kit until no retained set names it in its `data_key_ids`: restic keeps sets for about 3 months (7 daily, 4 weekly, 3 monthly), so for 3 months and a week after the rotation's verification pass (Deviation 31).
  9. **Restore** on a fresh host: host setup, `docker compose up -d postgres`, `docker compose --profile restore run --rm restore` (it refuses a non-empty database; exports are not in a backup set, so they come back `expired`, or `purged` when a later deletion named them, and the user requests a new one), its two verification outputs and the set's `data_key_ids`; then, before `up -d`, one pass per key id of the set other than the kit's current key: that key from the kit installed as `data_key_previous`, then `docker compose run --rm --no-deps worker jobhunter crypto rotate` (exit 3 `other_keys_remain` names the key ids still present; exit 0 once only the current key remains); then `data_key_previous` emptied and `up -d`; the RTO of 4 h is measured by the deployment step's timed restore.
  10. **Upgrade and rollback**: step 0 check out the release tag (R24), then §19.6's steps; rollback redeploys the previous tag when its `migrate` reports the schema `ahead_compatible`, and restores the last backup set when it reports `migrate_schema_ahead_incompatible` (Deviation 32).
  11. **Disk pressure**, **password lost** (§19.8).
  12. **The embedder model**: the VPS pulls the release's embedder image and never builds it, so it needs no route to `huggingface.co`; a host that must build it stages the files of `embedder/download_model.sh` on a host that has the route (M1 Carry-forward 6).
  13. **The deployment step** (Carry-forward 1's procedure, as a checklist).
  - The existing E2E section's last sentence reads that the sentinel scans the retained traces and reports, not uploaded artefacts (M4 Ruling F4-d, R2-M3).

  10. **§17.7** (Deviation 9): the fast and durable ping paths, the manual-run rule, the UUID form, the `deadman_state` sync, no alert on a failed ping; *"pings and the period sync serialise on one lock, and no ping is sent while the schedule is disabled or the check paused"*.
  11. **§17.8** (Deviation 10): *"`hide_inputs=True`, `hide_outputs=True` and one anonymizer that keeps the metadata allowlist plus the token counts of `usage_metadata` and rewrites errors to `<ErrorClass>:<code>`; tracing is on in the prod compose only, unsampled"*.
  12. **§18.2, §18.7, §18.8** (Deviations 14, 28, 40, 41): a test per fence consumer; E2E stays on the dev stack, E18 with the shipper and a local restic repository; the compose test stack runs N1, N2, C32, C37, C39, V20, V22 and the drills in the `prodstack` job; `images` scans; the embedder image job; the release workflow.
  13. **§19.1–19.2** (Deviations 29, 33, 34, 39, 47, 55): §19.1's outline as `deploy/compose.yaml` holds it (each correction of Deviation 33, the image variables of `release.lock`; `migrate` and `restore` mount `exports` read-write); §19.2's env delivery, `JOBHUNTER_BACKUP_PRUNE_CRON` (Sunday 02:30 UTC), `RESTIC_CACHE_DIR`, the restore-test default `30 3 * * 0` on the first Sunday only, `data_key_previous` always declared.
  14. **§19.3–19.4** (Deviations 32, 37, 38, 46): the compatibility floor; step 5 of `migrate` (L6201): *"…then the identities advance once more, over every table, now that the queue's and the checkpointer's schemas exist: on a first installation their sequences are created here, after steps 1 and 3"*, and step 1's and step 3's "identities" also cover `serial` columns and every id a manifest lists; *"identities advance with `setval` whenever the sequence's next allocatable value (`last_value`, plus one once called) is at or below the bound, never lowered, skipping tables above every bound"*; `migrate --db-role`; the commands `backup …`, `restore run`, `restore verify` in the `restore` container, `manifests replay`, `crypto rotate` as the worker, `ops …`, `startup-check --role <service>`.
  15. **§19.5** (Deviations 16, 29, 30, 31, 33, 55, 56): *"a selector names only properties a row never loses; any other membership is an id list frozen when the manifest is computed, and a state set on a kept row is checked relative to the bounds, so a completed manifest stays satisfied under later activity; a replay makes no external call"*; *"a backup set holds no export archive; after replay, a restore expires the exports still `ready`, fails those still `running`, empties the exports volume and removes its staging copy of the dump; a replayed purge removes export archives as the live one does, and an export already `failed`, `expired` or `purged` satisfies it"*; a manifest's `admitted_at` (A0's clock, its audit writes) beside `fenced_at` (the commit cutoff, its bounds); the backup jobs as `jobhunter backup` commands, the scheduler failing rows a dead container left `running`; restic's host, tags, `forget --tag set`, `--retry-lock`, explicit `init`; the shipper ships on change; the set snapshots `/data/staging/set` and its `backup_set.json` lists `data_key_ids`; every restore names its subfolder (`snapshot:subfolder`); *"an empty manifest stream (no purge yet) restores as empty; a shipped manifest missing from the stream stops the restore"*; the restore orchestration (the operator starts postgres; `restore run` refuses a non-empty database); L6304: *"Recovery is a fresh VPS, the offline recovery kit, `docker compose`, the restore order above, and `up`; the RTO is measured by the deployment's timed restore"*.
  16. **§19.6–19.7** (Deviations 32, 42, 47): rollback by compatibility floor or by restore; deploy step 0; the release workflow; §19.7's embedder figures for fp32 (about 2.6 GB RSS, about 0.6 docs/s on the dev CPU), the fp16 fallback removed (V7 measured it slower).
  17. **§19.8** (Deviations 31, 35, 37, 39): L6360 `docker compose restart worker`; L6362 *"then `docker compose restart` the services that receive it; a DB role's password also changes in PostgreSQL (`scripts/rotate-db-role.sh`)"*; host setup through `scripts/host-setup.sh`; `startup-check --role <service>`; the data-key rotation keeps the retired key in the recovery kit while a retained backup set names it; *"`crypto rotate` re-encrypts what the mounted previous key opens and reports the other key ids left, so a set restored under several old keys takes one pass per key, before `up -d`"*.
  18. **§20.1** (Deviations 18, 25, 26, 48): exports *"7 days; deleted by every purge but the nightly job tombstoning; rows 30 days after expiry"*; tombstoning and profile purges as `retention_daily` rules with their eligibility; backup records; `raw_items` *"a `deferred` item is kept until it is resolved"* (L2923, P26).
  19. **§20.2** (Deviations 14, 15, 17, 26, 50, 53, 54): the consumer table's new rows; *"a manifest row keeps its request, from which any later operation of the purge computes its content"*; *"a call whose content a purge redacted keeps it redacted: a late settlement records the usage and cost but no output"*; *"a global fence also stops new operations at their claim and the api's writes (409 `delete_everything_in_progress`), except login, logout, the privacy routes and the purge retry; every transaction of a request the edge let through reads the fence first"*; *"the completing transaction releases the fences, then checks the postconditions; a violation rolls both back"*; the shared job-graph cleanup (the targets first, at most 200 a transaction; merge events, conflicts; the job leaving its group with every co-member locked, which dissolves or re-elects and settles the deferred targets; merged jobs deepest first); identity resolution's row reads *"a fenced job in `P` or `J` defers the item (`deferred('job_fenced')`) until release"* (P25); the purge lifecycle of Deviation 17.
  20. **§20.3** (Deviations 19, 52): Task 17's mechanics in one paragraph, with *"an export starts reading only while no manifest is executing, waiting without spending an attempt for at most 24 hours, ends `failed` with its files removed whenever its operation fails, and ends `purged` when a manifest opened after its snapshot exists"*.
  21. **§20.4** (Deviations 2, 3, 24, 51): *"The user types `DELETE EVERYTHING`, re-enters the password and, with TOTP on, a code"*; step 2's mapping settles every online entry still `reserved` or `dispatched` before its call is deleted; kept: *"the user account and its TOTP enrolment"*; step 5's `duplicate_groups`; step 8: *"a dedicated reset installs the default settings version (no activation gate, no tuple, no operation) and re-points `active_config` and `schedule_state`"*; step 11's login attempts, lockouts and finished procrastinate jobs, and the request's own idempotency row; step 2's `submitted`/`ended` row: *"a live purge sends the cancel itself (3 tries, each a retrieval and a cancel, then logged) before step 3 deletes the submission; a replay sends none"*; step 7: *"after one durable dead-man ping pass"*; step 11: *"the alerts resolved when the manifest is computed, an id list (the others are kept)"*; step 8's postcondition relative to the manifest's bounds; step 12: *"sessions created up to the manifest are deleted in the final transaction"*.
  22. **§20.5** (Deviations 3, 23): the disabling settings version, the in-flight batches, the embeddings, the listed golden cases, the re-authentication; *"a replay re-applies the disabling version, `purge_fence_at` and the run cancels, unless a settings version created after the fence re-enabled Adzuna"*.
  23. **§20.6** (Deviations 18, 27): the Operator section; the export note.
  24. **§22, §23** (Deviations 1, 36): V15 and V21 at the deployment step with M5's CLI and stack test; V20 and V22 recorded; §23's domain row *"an A record"*; "Proxy network subnet" becomes *"Network subnets (`proxy`, `edge`, `internal`, `egress`) and Caddy's two addresses"*; the backup target row's versioning note.

---

## Deviations

Each deviation settles something the spec leaves open, departs from it, or stages it. Entries that change user-visible behaviour say so. Revision 3.7 of the spec (Task 32) carries every one that changes spec text; the item number is given. Where two research digests proposed different mechanisms, the entry names the one chosen and why (M5-R1).

1. **The rehearsal is local; the VPS deployment is a later step** (M5-U1). M5 runs the full production stack locally and in CI (`prodstack`) with the backup, restore, upgrade-and-rollback and rotation drills and N1/N2; real ACME, V7 and V21 on the VPS, the timed restore from the recovery kit and V15 with real accounts are the deployment step (Carry-forward 1), for which M5 delivers the deploy checklist, `scripts/host-setup.sh` and the V15 CLI. Spec: 3.7 item 24.
2. **Delete everything keeps the account and its TOTP enrolment** (M5-U3). It deletes `login_attempts` and `login_lockouts` (they hold IP addresses). User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 item 21.
3. **Re-authentication with the TOTP code** (M5-U4). Delete-everything and the source purge need the password and, with TOTP on, a code; each failure is a failed login attempt in the §16.3 throttle, so five lock the address out. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 6, 7, 8, 21, 22.
4. **The ntfy `Title` is ASCII** (O3). `Job Hunter - <code>`: httpx encodes header values as ASCII, so the spec's em dash would fail every send. Spec: 3.7 item 9.
5. **The dispatcher and its outbox** (Q1, Q2, Q4, Q5, O8, O9, O22a). The `alerts` row is the outbox; the worker's asyncio loop pushes by the row's severity at or above `notifications.push_min_severity`, never `info`, never `notify.delivery_failed`, never a resolution; at most ten per pass, criticals first; four tries (5, 30, 120 s) for the first failing alert of a pass, then a pause of 5 min doubling to 60 min; `Click` points at `/settings#alerts`, where the list is. Spec: 3.7 item 9.
6. **Escalation and manual resolution** (O6, O7, M4 Carry-forward 4). One dedup key rises from warn to critical (and is pushed again at once), never back; `POST /api/alerts/{id}/resolve` resolves a catalogue code marked manual; acknowledging `llm.reservation_bound_violated` resolves it, so its next occurrence pauses the lane and pushes again. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 6, 9.
7. **Catalogue rows for the codes the code raises** (O19, M2 Carry-forward 3). `llm.unexpected_stop_reason`, `profile.extract_generation_mismatch`, `dedup.ambiguous_grouping`, `quota.serpapi_renewal_stale`, `reconciler.failed`, `retention.failed`, `api.unhandled_exception`, `auth.totp_key_mismatch`, and the new `docparse.leftover` (a marker file docparse leaves before exit 70). Spec: 3.7 item 9.
8. **The never-raised codes and the missing auto-resolves** (O11, O12, O17, Q9, Q11, Q18, M1 Carry-forward 11). `worker.heartbeat_stale` from an api lifespan task with a 120 s start grace; `system.*` from `metrics_refresh`; `embedder.checksum_mismatch` best effort (the real embedder exits 78, so a mismatch surfaces as readiness and the dead-man alarm); `scoring.canary_drift`, `batch.submit_ambiguous_unresolved` and `dedup.ambiguous_grouping` resolve when their condition ends. Spec: 3.7 item 9.
9. **The dead-man mechanisms** (Q6–Q8, O2, O5, O10, O18; M5-R2). Pings on a fast path after the run-ending commit and a durable tick path above `deadman_state.last_pinged_run_id` (which also covers `resume_exhausted` and `orphaned_queue`); a manual run whose window covered a skipped scheduled slot pings with its own status; the ping URL must be the UUID form (startup check); the tick defers `deadman_sync` when the applied period differs from `schedule_state`, with a 5 → 60 min backoff and `deadman.update_failed`; a failed ping raises nothing; pings and `deadman_sync` serialise on the advisory lock 7261005, and no ping is sent while the schedule is disabled or the check paused, so a run ending after a disable never resumes the check. User-visible: yes (no false alarm after Run now). Spec: 3.7 items 8, 9, 10.
10. **LangSmith's client** (Q15–Q17, O13–O16, O21; M5-R2). On in the prod compose only, unsampled; `hide_inputs=True`, `hide_outputs=True` and one anonymizer (in langsmith 0.14.11 an anonymizer replaces a callable `hide_*`), which keeps the metadata allowlist plus `usage_metadata`'s token counts and rewrites errors to `<ErrorClass>:<code>`; the startup check also requires the key and refuses replica endpoints, key variables and a sampling rate; the worker publishes its tracing state in `worker_heartbeat.tracing` for the health page; `langsmith` becomes a direct pin. Spec: 3.7 items 3, 8, 9, 11.
11. **ntfy and healthchecks configuration** (O4). `NTFY_TOPIC_URL` and `HEALTHCHECKS_API_URL` are loaded by exact name, the topic URL is masked in logs, and a prod worker needs an https topic (`ntfy_in_prod`). Spec: 3.7 item 8.
12. **Backup alerts come from the worker** (O1, O20; M5-R2). The backup role cannot write `alerts`, so the `backup_health` reconciler derives `backup.failed`, `backup.stale` (warn, escalated) and `backup.restore_test_failed` from the backup tables (and the age gauge); "install" is the first settings version; the restore-test age is a health state without an alert. Spec: 3.7 items 9, 15.
13. **A purge's in-batch targets** (P1; M5-R3; Known open point 1 closed; M3 Ruling P42). A purge abandons a `preparing` submission; for a later one it requests the submission's cancel (§20.2) and cancels the purged targets individually (§10.10), settles their items' entries `ambiguous`, and records in `purged_batch_items` (ids only) each deleted item whose submission can still return results (`submit_started` to `ended`, and `released`, which the poll and the adoption still read), whatever its target's state: the positive evidence on which the result reader skips a line as `purged` (any other line without an item stays foreign) and the ambiguous adoption still matches the original membership, so the batch completes; a fenced item stores no output. User-visible: no. Spec: 3.7 items 1, 4, 5.
14. **Fence coverage** (P2, D21). `det_completeness`, the scope precompute, `record_feedback`, `set_flags`, group-wide actions, split, the embedding writers and the target actions honour the fence; the jobs list omits fenced jobs. Spec: 3.7 item 19.
15. **The global fence as an admission stop** (P3, D15; M5-R3). The operation claim leaves a non-purge operation `pending` with `not_before` and no attempt spent; the api's writes answer 409 `delete_everything_in_progress` except login, logout, the privacy routes and the purge retry (the only recovery of a failed delete-everything, whose global fence stays), matched by method and path before routing, since the route template is not known yet; every transaction a guarded request begins reads the global fence as its first statement (an engine `begin` hook keyed by a context variable the edge sets), so a request that passed the edge before the fence committed writes nothing after it, a CV upload included (it takes no `active_config`); a read-only snapshot runs outside the guard, since its isolation level must be set before any query and it can write nothing; the guard lives in `db/fence_guard.py`, so `db/snapshot.py` uses it without importing the api. Chosen over per-route re-checks under `active_config`, which would have to be found and kept for every write route. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 6, 19.
16. **Manifest format and replay semantics** (D2, D3, D4, D16, D17). Canonical JSON with `format: 1` and a top-level `admitted_at` (A0's clock, the value of every audit field A0 writes) and `fenced_at` (the later commit cutoff that bounds others' rows) in every manifest; tables without an identity column bounded by their parent's id or by `created_at ≤ fenced_at`; `login_lockouts`, which has neither (and whose key is an IP address), gains an identity `generation` renewed by every lock, its bound; a check of A0's own writes compares with `admitted_at`, a bound on other rows with `fenced_at`; replay re-runs the kind's procedure on the stored ids (which recreates tombstones); live deletion is bounded by `max_ids` as replay is; `retention_purge` has no writer. Every postcondition stays true under later normal activity (plan review round 9), since §19.5 replays a completed manifest whose postcondition fails. Selectors name only immutable properties. Every mutable membership (alerts open or resolved, procrastinate jobs `doing` or not, references, kept rows) is frozen at A2 as ids. A state set on a kept row is checked relative to the bounds and to the manifest's frozen `retained` ids. Tables without an identity column are bounded by a parent, by `created_at`, or by the owner a checkpoint's `thread_id` names. Every id a manifest lists is one identity advancement moves past, `serial` columns included. Spec: 3.7 item 15.
17. **The purge operation's lifecycle** (D1, D5, D6, D7). One purge at a time (`OPERATION_LOCKS`), dedup key `purge:<uuid>`; the quiesce predicate over `pg_stat_activity` with a 10 min bound; the retry route inserts a new operation; the reconciler alerts on a failed purge but re-queues only a manifest with no live or failed operation, and retention keeps purge operations of `executing` manifests; a failure hook alerts at once; a crash after the file's rename adopts the written file instead of recomputing it; each chunk locks in §3.3.3's order (epoch, configuration, job rows with their groups' co-members when asked, owner); the completing transaction releases the fences, then checks the postconditions, and rolls back on a violation. Spec: 3.7 items 9, 15, 19.
18. **Exports and purges** (M5-R3; digest U3). Every manifest-producing purge deletes every export except the nightly job tombstoning; PRIVACY.md says an export can hold content tombstoned after it was made until it expires (7 days). User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 18, 20, 23.
19. **Export mechanics** (D9, chosen over a whole-blob encryption that neither O-ops nor R-prod specified). A status JSON and a `?download=1` stream (410 `gone` once expired or purged); 1 MiB segments each AES-256-GCM-encrypted with the data key under AAD `export:<id>:<n>:<last>`, so the api streams within its memory limit and a truncated or reordered file fails; one live export (a unique index); `exports.size_bytes`; the export waits while any manifest is `executing` and records the purge generation (`max(deletion_manifests.id)`) its snapshot saw, and ends `purged` at publication when a manifest above it exists, so a purge without a fence (the golden-case delete) cannot leak into it either; the download is not passphrase-protected (M5-R3, digest U4); it opens its file before the 200 starts, so a purge racing it answers 410 instead of a 500, and a download already under way completes from its open file. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 4, 6, 20.
20. **The golden-case delete answers 202** (U8, P10). The api has no `manifests` volume, so the route opens a `golden_case_delete` purge and the worker writes the file. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 item 6.
21. **Re-authentication errors and retries** (D13; M5-U4). A wrong phrase is 422 `confirmation_mismatch` (nothing recorded); a wrong password or code is 403 `reauth_failed`. The idempotency fingerprint of the three privacy POSTs leaves out `password` and `totp`, and a completed request's stored response is returned before re-authentication, so a lost response can be retried after its TOTP step was used. Spec: 3.7 item 6.
22. **Deletion statuses after a logout and failed purges** (D8, D18, P24). `GET /api/privacy/manifests` lists them (the single GET gains `id`, `kind`, `created_at` and a source purge's deleted golden cases); Needs attention lists failed purges with Retry. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 6, 7.
23. **The Adzuna purge's mechanics** (D10, D22; digest U6). Adzuna is disabled through a new internal settings version; in-flight batches are cancelled per Deviation 13; the embeddings of deleted tainted revisions are deleted; the manifest lists the deleted golden cases. A replay re-applies A0's durable effects before `execute` (`purge_fence_at` never lowered; the disabling settings version unless the active one was created after `admitted_at`, the user's later choice; the runs queued up to `admitted_at` cancelled), and the kind's postconditions check the first two against `admitted_at`, the one value A0 wrote them with. Spec: 3.7 item 22.
24. **Delete-everything's table list and sessions** (P4, P19, D14; the M2 final review's running proposal). Every table is either deleted or in `DELETE_EVERYTHING_KEEPS`; a dedicated `reset_to_defaults` (no activation gate, no tuple, no operation) installs the default settings version, and `schedule_state` follows it; `duplicate_groups`, finished procrastinate jobs, login attempts and lockouts go; the request's own idempotency row stays, so a retried key returns the stored 202; sessions created up to the manifest are deleted in the final transaction; a running proposal of a cancelled operation fails. Nothing it deletes is still needed by a side effect in flight (plan review round 8): it sends the recorded batch cancels itself before step 3 deletes the submissions; it runs the dead-man's durable ping pass before step 7 deletes the runs; and step 11 keeps the alerts open at A2, the dispatcher's outbox and the score lane's pause gates, which hold codes and ids only. The deleted alerts and procrastinate jobs are id lists frozen at A2, step 10's scope versions too (leaving out the rows the reset reuses), and step 8's postcondition is relative to the bounds (plan review round 9), so a later alert resolution, a finished job, a settings save or a profile activation never makes a later `migrate` re-run the purge. Plan review round 10 adds two things. `api_idempotency` gains a surrogate identity `id`, so the request's own row is kept by `keep=[idempotency_row_id]`, an id the request carries. Each `active_config` and `schedule_state` pointer may also name a row of the manifest's frozen `retained` set: the scope versions step 10 keeps, and the rows of the kept policy tables, which the reset and later saves reuse by hash. Spec: 3.7 items 4, 21.
25. **Tombstoning and profile-purge triggers** (D11, D12, P15). `retention_daily` opens tombstone batches of up to 200 jobs (ten per run) and re-checks the exemptions under the fence; `tombstone_keys` is upserted; a profile purge never takes the active or previous version or a synthetic eval version; its eligibility is read in A0's transaction under `active_config FOR UPDATE`, and every writer that can make a version ineligible holds `active_config` (golden-case activation now takes it `FOR SHARE` and refuses a purged or fenced profile; plan review round 7), so eligibility cannot change between admission and the computed content. Spec: 3.7 item 18.
26. **Two spec corrections** (P25, P26). Identity resolution defers an item of a fenced job (`deferred('job_fenced')`, as L2918/L3277 and the code), and a `deferred` raw item is kept until resolved. Spec: 3.7 items 18, 19.
27. **The privacy statement in the app** (D20; M5-R3). The SPA renders `docs/PRIVACY.md` at build time as React elements (no route, no HTML injection); its Operator section holds five marked fields that the deploy checklist fills. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 items 7, 23.
28. **Shipping on the dev and E2E stacks; S3 on the test stack** (D19 vs R-prod §6 item 17; M5-R4 R29). The dev and E2E stacks gain `manifests` and `exports` volumes and an on-demand `shipper` with a local restic repository (E18 needs `shipped`, and the dev disk cannot afford an S3 server); the S3 code path is exercised once, on the compose test stack, against versitygw, the smallest pinned S3 server found (about 36 MB). Spec: 3.7 item 12.
29. **The backup image and its jobs** (R3, R17; R-prod §6 items 1–2). Built from the app runtime (the `Dockerfile`'s named `restic` and `pgclient` source stages, since `COPY --from` takes no variable; every app build names `target: runtime`) with the PostgreSQL 17 client copied from the pinned pgvector image (no download; client and server one build) and restic 0.19.1 from its image; the cron runner is `jobhunter backup scheduler` (croniter, already locked): busybox `crond` needs root to switch users and Debian's applet was not verifiable offline; backup and restore run as 10001 in group 10010 under the x-app hardening instead of root (they own the CV files and read the 0440 secrets by group); the jobs are typed `jobhunter backup …` commands instead of shell scripts; their staging directory is configuration (`JOBHUNTER_STAGING_DIR`), and the scheduler fails at its start any row a dead container left `running`. Spec: 3.7 items 2, 3, 13, 15.
30. **restic conventions** (R13, R14, R15). `--host jobhunter-backup`, tags `set` and `set:<id>`, `forget --tag set`, a weekly prune cron (`JOBHUNTER_BACKUP_PRUNE_CRON`, Sunday 02:30 UTC), `--retry-lock 30m`, a cache directory, `restic init` explicit; the set snapshots `/data/staging/set` (not the cache); restores name their subfolder (`snapshot:subfolder`) so files land at exact paths; an empty manifest stream (no purge yet) restores as empty, a shipped manifest missing from it stops the restore; the shipper ships only when the stream changed (the latest snapshot stays the complete stream); the restore test's cron is `30 3 * * 0`, run only on the first Sunday. Spec: 3.7 items 13, 15.
31. **The offline recovery kit** (R1; M5-R4). It holds the current `data_key` and every earlier data key a retained backup set still names (each set's `backup_set.json` lists the `data_key_ids` of its ciphertexts; a retired key stays about 3 months after its rotation), `restic_password`, the S3 keys, `RESTIC_REPOSITORY` and the env file; a restore of a set from before a rotation runs, before `up -d`, one `crypto rotate` pass per key id the set names other than the current key, each key mounted in turn as `data_key_previous`: `rotate` re-encrypts what that key opens and exits 3 `other_keys_remain` while other keys remain, so two key slots recover a set under any number of old keys; the RUNBOOK has its section. Spec: 3.7 items 8, 15, 17.
32. **Rollback by compatibility floor** (R2; M5-R4). `schema_compat` records each revision's floor; an older release's `migrate` and `/readyz` accept a newer head whose floor they know and refuse a destructive or unknown one (`migrate_schema_ahead_incompatible`), which the RUNBOOK rolls back by restore; migration 0011 is the drill's real expand migration. Spec: 3.7 items 4, 14, 16.
33. **The production compose's choices** (R5, R10, R16, R19, R20, R26, R28). `init: true`, the TCP postgres healthcheck, `proxy`'s `ip_range`, `jobhunter api` as the api command; all four subnets and Caddy's two addresses pinned; Caddy keeps the image's user with every capability dropped but `NET_BIND_SERVICE` (its persistent ACME state stays owned by that user without a derived image), read-only, 256 MB; embedder and docparse as in dev; memory limits for backup, migrate and restore; each service's env by `${NAME:?}` interpolation; the operator starts postgres before `restore run`, which refuses a non-empty database; every named volume has one owner and mode, carried by the mount point of every image that mounts it (Task 21's table: the `backup` stage adds `/data/restic-repo`, `s3stub` runs as root), so no start order can leave a volume root-owned under a non-root writer. Spec: 3.7 items 13, 15.
34. **The Caddyfile and the login limit** (R-prod §6 item 7; M4 Ruling T17-b). One `site.caddy` for the headers, used by the prod and test Caddyfiles; no access log; HTTP/3 off; stock Caddy has no rate limiter, so the api's edge refuses more than 10 logins a minute per address (100 in all). User-visible: no. Spec: 3.7 items 8, 13.
35. **The egress rule's mechanism** (R9). An own nft table on the forward hook, loaded by a systemd unit before Docker, dropping new connections from the `proxy`, `internal` and `edge` subnets to non-container addresses (Caddy's `edge` address excepted); the file keeps its §16.9 name. Spec: 3.7 items 2, 8, 17.
36. **An A record only** (R8). An AAAA record would put every IPv6 client behind one bridge address in the login throttle; IPv6 is Carry-forward 2. Spec: 3.7 item 24.
37. **Backup and restore roles** (R4, R22). Startup roles `backup` and `restore` with their secrets and a DB-role selector for the DSN; `startup-check` always names its role. Spec: 3.7 items 8, 14, 17.
38. **`migrate`'s flags and the identity advancement** (R12, R25). `--database`, `--steps sequences,alembic,replay`, `--manifest-dir`, `--cv-dir`, `--db-role`; identities advance with `setval`, never lowered, skipping tables above every bound, so a deploy's `migrate` takes no exclusive table lock; the decision reads the sequence's next allocatable value (`is_called` counts: a fresh sequence's next value is its `last_value`); `run_migrate` stays the synchronous wrapper of its existing callers and `run_migrate_async` takes the same flags for the restore code. Step 5 ends with one more advancement over every table (plan review round 11): on a first installation the queue's sequences are created only there, after steps 1 and 3, so without it a new queue job could take an id an old manifest lists. Spec: 3.7 item 14.
39. **Rotation** (R6, R7). `docker compose restart` after a secret file changes (Compose does not see file contents); a DB role's password also changes in PostgreSQL through `scripts/rotate-db-role.sh`, whose compose command line and secrets directory default to production's and are options, so the drill rotates the test project's role and nothing else; `data_key_previous` is always declared (empty outside a rotation). Spec: 3.7 items 13, 17.
40. **The image pipeline** (R11; M0 Deviation 15). An `images` job builds without pushing and scans with trivy; the real embedder image builds only when `embedder/` changes; `release.yml` runs on dispatch only; `prodstack` runs on pull requests to `main` and on demand. Spec: 3.7 item 12.
41. **Test placement** (R29; M5-R4). E2E stays on the dev stack (§18.7, revision 3.6; M4 Carry-forward 2's re-pointing is dropped); N1, N2, C32, C37, C39, V20, V22 and the drills run on the compose test stack in `prodstack`, not in `e2e`; E18 runs on the E2E stack. Spec: 3.7 item 12.
42. **The embedder stays fp32** (R21). §19.1/§19.7's int8 figures become fp32's, and the fp16 fallback goes (V7 measured it slower); fp32 or int8 is decided after V7 on the VPS. Spec: 3.7 items 3, 16.
43. **`manifest_shipping` runs every minute** (the tick cadence; §17.4 says 5 min), because the cadences are start, tick, heavy and daily and the query is cheap. Spec: 3.7 item 9.
44. **Health additions** (M4 Carry-forward 5; O16, O20). The §17.5 backup, shipment, unshipped-manifest and restore-test items as states; LangSmith's state as the worker published it. User-visible: yes. Spec: 3.7 item 9.
45. **Modules outside §4.2** (marked *(new)* in File Structure): `db/compat.py`, `observability/{catalogue,heartbeat_watch,backup_health}.py`, `privacy/{reconcilers.py,kinds/}`, `backup/`, `cli_ops.py`, `cli_backup.py`, `crypto_rotate.py`, `api/reauth.py`, `db/fence_guard.py`, the deploy files of Tasks 21–22. Spec: 3.7 item 3.
46. **Where the restore commands run** (R23). `restore verify` and the migrate subset run in the `restore` container (and in `backup` for the restore test), which mount the staging volume and restic; `migrate` keeps `manifests replay`. Spec: 3.7 item 14.
47. **Release digests in `deploy/release.lock`** (§19.6; R24). The release PR writes the four image digests into `deploy/release.lock`, which the prod command passes with `--env-file`, so `compose.yaml` stays stable and the test overlay can replace them; deploys start by checking out the release tag. Spec: 3.7 items 13, 16.
48. **Export expiry** (§20.1). A `ready` export becomes `expired` after 7 days with its file removed; terminal rows go 30 days after expiry. Spec: 3.7 item 18.
49. **`query_proposals` reaches `purged`** (§7.5, §7.16). The status existed without an edge; `running | ready | failed → purged` join the registry, `items` nulled in the same update, a running proposal's operation cancelled first. Spec: 3.7 item 4.
50. **The job-graph deletion's order and locks** (§20.2 L6451, L6486; plan review round 2). A job graph's targets go first, in transactions of at most `TARGET_SET_CHUNK` (200) each, as L6451 chunks them (`delete_target_set` refuses more, and a graph has no bound); then one transaction deletes the graph. That transaction locks the graph's jobs and every co-member of their duplicate groups in one ascending-id statement before the operation owner, and starts again when a member joined under the lock. The job then leaves its group through `groups.leave_group`, which dissolves or re-elects and settles the group's deferred targets, not through `representative.reelect`, which does neither the lock check nor the settlement. Tombstoning, the Adzuna purge and delete-everything share it. Spec: 3.7 item 19.
51. **A purge settles the online spend of the calls it deletes** (G04; plan review round 3). Every reader of an online ledger entry joins its `llm_calls` row (`llm/spend_ledger.py:583, 1881`), so an entry left `reserved` or `dispatched` when a purge deletes its call could never be settled or recovered. Before any purge deletes such a call (target-set deletion, the Adzuna purge's eval calls, the golden-case delete, delete-everything), `settle_for_purge` moves `reserved` → `not_billed('never_dispatched')` and `dispatched` → `ambiguous` (counted at its reservation), in the schedule's lock order; the late response of a purged call, or its fenced dispatch marker, settles nothing and raises nothing; a postcondition checks that no unsettled entry lacks its call. User-visible: no. Spec: 3.7 items 5, 21.
52. **Operations that wait spend no attempt; an export never outlives its operation** (plan review round 3). `OperationContext.wait_in` returns a claimed operation to `pending` without an attempt, for waits with their own end: an export waiting out a purge (at most 24 hours, then `failed`) and a golden-case delete waiting out a running eval. The export's failure hook moves its row `running → failed` and removes its files whenever its operation ends `failed`, and retention fails a `running` export left without a live operation, so `exports_one_running` never blocks the next export. User-visible: yes (an export can end `failed`). Spec: 3.7 item 20.
53. **A manifest row keeps its request** (plan review round 4). §7.14's `deletion_manifests` gains `request jsonb` (ids only), written at A0; a purge operation's payload is `{manifest_id}` alone. An operation can be lost before the content exists: the `purge_fences` reconciler's replacement, the retry route's new operation, delete-everything's cancel and deletion of another purge's operation, a pending retry completed by replay. Each computes the content from the row, so a golden-case delete, which has no fence that names its case, is never left `executing` with nothing to compute from. User-visible: no. Spec: 3.7 items 4, 19.
54. **Redacted call content stays redacted** (plan review round 4). `llm_calls.content_purged_at` marks a `field_redact llm_calls output_json`. `spend_ledger.settle` writes `output_json` through a SQL `CASE` on that marker, evaluated on the row it locks, so a provider response that arrives after a profile purge records its usage and cost but never its content. The redaction covers every call of the version, published or not, and that set is closed at admission (plan review round 5): A0 cancels the version's live `propose_queries` operations, and every reservation and dispatch marker requires its operation `running` under its claim. Every proposal operation is created under `active_config` (the proposal route takes it `FOR SHARE` before reading the active version; the confirm and activation hold it `FOR UPDATE`; plan review round 6), so none can be created for the version after A0. No call of the version can be created after A0, and A2's set is final. Chosen over a profile-fence check at reservation, which would need the version in every owner's lock path. User-visible: no. Spec: 3.7 items 4, 19.
55. **Export archives across restore and replay** (plan review round 4). §19.1 mounts `exports` in neither `migrate` nor `restore`, so neither could remove an archive. Both now mount it read-write, and a replayed purge runs its export ops as the live one does, against the mounted volume or the restore test's scratch directory. A backup set holds no archive: each is the user's content and lives 7 days. Export states follow one table (Task 17) for live purges, replay and restore: `failed`, `expired` and `purged` are content-free and satisfy a purge's export op as they are; only `ready` and `running` rows move. A restore applies its column after replay (plan review round 5): exports a replayed manifest names end `purged`, the rest still `ready` end `expired` (410 `gone`) and the rest still `running` end `failed`; then the volume is emptied. It removes its staging copy of the dump whatever the outcome. The export handler removes the files of an export that a purge or a restore ended, and of its own earlier attempts; retention removes any archive no `ready` row names. User-visible: yes (an export from before a restore is expired). Spec: 3.7 items 13, 15.
56. **A purge's external effects are live only** (plan review round 9). Two effects call outside the database: delete-everything's provider cancel of a recorded batch (`send_purge_cancel`, three tries, each a retrieval and a cancel while `in_progress`, then `batch_cancel_gave_up`), and its dead-man ping pass. Both happen only in a live purge, through `PurgeRun.effects`, which `run_purge` builds from the worker's `OperationDeps`. Replay passes `None`: it builds no Batches client and no pinger and needs no Anthropic or healthchecks secret, so `migrate`, `restore` and the monthly restore test keep their roles' secrets and never touch real provider work. In replay a recorded cancel is dropped and `batch_cancel_skipped_replay` logged with its ids. The cost: when a restored dump holds a batch whose live cancel was never sent, at most that batch's spend. That spend is already reserved or `ambiguous` in the kept ledger, and the provider expires the batch within 24 hours. No other kind reaches an external call from `execute`: ntfy pushes are the dispatcher's, the shipper and restic are the backup container's, and embeddings are the worker's later work. User-visible: no. Spec: 3.7 items 15, 21.

## Carry-forward

1. **The deployment step, with the user** (M5-U1). In this order, each recorded as passed, blocked (by what) or not attempted (why), in a `docs/spikes/V21.md` and `V15.md` update and a deployment record:
   1. Provision the VPS (EU, 4 vCPU with AVX-512 VNNI preferred, 16 GB, ≥ 100 GB NVMe, LUKS where offered, no swap or encrypted swap, the pinned Docker Engine version); SSH key-only.
   2. Fill the deploy checklist's inputs; PRIVACY.md's operator fields; the exclusivity confirmations (C2, C3).
   3. Run `release.yml`, merge the digest PR, check out the release tag on the VPS.
   4. `sudo scripts/host-setup.sh --secrets-from <dir> --delete-sources` (the egress unit loads at boot).
   5. DNS: an A record; Caddy against Let's Encrypt staging first (`acme_ca`), then production.
   6. `docker compose run --rm backup jobhunter backup init` against the real S3 bucket; `up -d`; `user create`; `/readyz` from outside.
   7. V21: N1 on the VPS image (api and docparse cannot resolve or connect out; the worker can; the rule loaded at boot).
   8. V20: N2 from two real client addresses.
   9. V7 on the VPS CPU: fp32 and int8 throughput, RSS and parity; the user decides the export (M1 Deviation 47); a new embedder image if int8.
   10. V15: `ops notify-test` arrives on the phone; `ops deadman-sync --show` shows the period; healthchecks' own alert route tested once.
   11. A first nightly backup set and a manifest shipment offsite; a manual `backup restore-test`.
   12. The timed restore: from the offline recovery kit only, onto a second fresh VPS (or the same one wiped), against the RTO of 4 h.
   13. One release deployed and rolled back.
   14. A first free-sources collection; a 24–72 h soak (backup age, dead-man pings, alert delivery, disk, memory).
2. **IPv6 (AAAA)** (R8): enable IPv6 on `edge` with the daemon's ip6tables NAT so Caddy sees real IPv6 peers, extend the egress rule to the IPv6 subnets, add an IPv6 case to N2; publish AAAA only then.
3. **After M5**, each with its reason:
   - procrastinate schema upgrades: M5 keeps 3.10.0; the next version bump adds its migration scripts to migrate step 5 (M0 Deviation 5, last part);
   - the remaining modules of M4 Carry-forward 3 (`profile/upload.py`, `queries/authoring.py`, `api_idempotency.py`): untouched by M5's tasks (`scoring/golden.py` and `observability/health.py` were converted where M5 touched them);
   - the embedder's poison-batch quarantine (M1 Carry-forward 11): no poison batch was observed, and it needs a schema field and the embed node's bisection, outside operations and privacy;
   - docparse isolation beyond its per-job directories (M2 Carry-forward 3, its isolation part): a per-job container is a redesign of §12.2; M5 keeps the tmpfs, the per-job directories, `pids_limit` and the leftover alert;
   - the activation preview's `unverified_pairs` and the German title (M4 Ruling F4-a): optional, no defect;
   - the keyboard-only marking path (spec Known open point 7) and the profile confirm's comparison with a repaired profile (M4 re-review 1): UI work outside M5's subsystems;
   - the 13 moderate npm advisories (M4 Ruling T19-d) and the five legacy scripts outside mypy (M4 T20-a): a dependency pass with new pins and a hygiene pass;
   - the c17/c29 crash-test flakes (M4 ledger): unrelated subsystems, passing in full runs;
   - `@playwright/test` 1.64.1 (M4 Carry-forward 2's last part): the sandbox browser stays chromium-1194;
   - V14's open question (M3), the Haiku first-stage budget, the Sonnet thinking tokens and `SIZING_INPUT_BOUND_TOKENS` (M3 findings; Known open point 6), the contact stripper's mixed header lines (Known open point 2; PRIVACY.md states the limit), JSearch's and Arbeitnow's page-cap rules (M1 Carry-forward 5, 7), Known open points 3 and 4: the user's model and product decisions, no M5 code.
4. **Not M5's** (M5-R6): M4's acceptance Part B (the controller runs it before M5); CI runners that start no job (a user action; Task 33 records whether CI ran); the session-start hook's `--group docparse` (the user's, M3 Ruling T28-d); PR #5's merge order (the controller's).
